105 quick-tier rows of the CI recording re-run to the same digests, verdicts, controls and
conflict counts (`check: ok (2,225 rows, 105 quick-tier rows re-run and reproduced)`). A first full run (discarded: it recorded `dirty_tree: true`, see the
lesson on provenance fields) agreed with this one on the instance digest, the
verdict and, for every UNSAT row, the conflict count, on all 1,572 rows that ended
by their own verdict; the 53 rows that differ either ended on the 120 s wall (a
function of machine speed, as it must be) or are the ten E2 rows whose
expectation the second run corrected.

**What decided it.** Row 1 holds when a *required* shape is not green in its worst
seed at the finest decomposition tested (section 9.11). Two are not, **but neither
measures what its name says**; the narrower, supported reading follows them:

* **C1 at 200 instructions, 25% divergence.** C1 on a B base is built as
  `pub XOR (f_code(s) XOR f_ref(s))` for two secret copies: it contains **two B2
  equivalences by construction** and can never be greener than B2 (section 9.7). It
  is not code-against-code two-safety. It reaches the 1,000,000-conflict cap at 200
  instructions and is red from 25 (354,220 conflicts at 5%). C1 on the A1 base is
  **green at every N** (115 to 942 conflicts). And section 3.6 already says VOS-029
  delivers no two-safety on a real entry, so **C1 does not gate VOS-029a**.
* **D3 at T = 8, k = 16.** In the two seeds that did not finish, the worst
  obligation reached 503,554 and 454,472 conflicts and no proof was produced
  (unmeasured); in the three that finished it is 242,607, 240,488 and 217,654
  conflicts with 37 to 43 MB of DRAT. The generated table shows the worst seed's own
  row (503,554 conflicts, no proof). "Two of five seeds `unknown`" is the **120 s
  row-sum wall** (VOS-D095: one row holds all eight obligations), not a
  per-obligation timeout. The identical obligation unsplit (D2, T = 8, k = 16) is
  184,385 to 503,554 conflicts and 31 to 116 MB of DRAT, two seeds rejected by the
  checker. With k = 8 it is green (at most 33,278 conflicts): the cliff is **chain
  depth** (D2: k = 8 is 10k to 28k conflicts, k = 16 is 184k to 504k).

**The supported finding is narrower than "X3 is infeasible":** an obligation that
chains more than about two to four non-structurally-matched 32-bit arithmetic
operations exceeds the checker's budget, and every obligation cut finer is green by
a factor of 60 or more (B3 with a cut after every instruction: worst step 1,654
conflicts against a budget of 100,000). It is a constraint on **cut density**, not
on X3 as a route. Row 1 is still the computed outcome of the procedure fixed before
the run, and it stays.

**What the table shows besides, and what it does not.**

* **Green, with a wide margin:** A1 and A2 at every N up to 64 (at most 547
  conflicts); B3 in certified mode **with a cut after every instruction**, at
  every length from 25 to 1,000 and at 5%, 25% and 100% divergence (the worst
  step is 1,654 conflicts at every length; 1,000 instructions at 100% divergence
  is 925,077 conflicts in 1,000 certified obligations); D4 (a cut after every
  access, the tables compared whole) through T = 16 at k up to 16; D2 (concrete
  offsets) at T of 16 or more for every k; B4 `x*5` and `base + i*12` at every
  width; `n`-term re-association up to n = 4.
* **Red:** monolithic B2 from 50 instructions at every divergence (and from 25 at
  100%), where it reaches the proof cap or the wall (the cliff of table B2); B3
  with a cut every 8 instructions at 1,000 instructions (a worst step of 108,397
  conflicts) and every 32 instructions from 25 instructions; `n`-term
  re-association from n = 5 (470,248 conflicts, rejected by the default checker
  limits; n = 6 and 7 reach the cap); D1 (the eq/ite chain, no split, no cut) from
  k = 4 at T of 8 or more (k = 8 at T = 4); D4 at T of 64 or more (one access compared on the whole table
  needs 104,487 conflicts at T = 64 and T = 256 does not finish); C2 at T of 16 or
  more by a small margin (110,229 and 112,084 conflicts).
* **Not measured, because a row has a 120 s wall:** a *split* or *chain* is one
  row, so the wall bounds the sum of its obligations: D3 at T of 64 or more and B3
  with a cut every 32 at 1,000 instructions end `unknown (timeout)` before every
  obligation has run. Section 8.3's feasibility total is 10 minutes; a larger
  budget for chains would resolve these (backlog VOS-D095).
* **What it means.** The compositional route is cheap *where an obligation holds
  at most a few chained, non-structurally-matched arithmetic operations*: with a
  cut after every instruction (state equality as the cut relation, which
  presupposes a model aligned with the instructions: the owned-compile lane of VER13
  section 4.3 item 1, where a source map gives cut points by construction) the worst
  obligation is 1,654 conflicts at every length. Cuts every 8 instructions are red
  on the long entries and every 32 from 25 instructions; the grid jumps from 1 to 8,
  so **cuts every 2 and 4 instructions were not measured**. **Not measured at all:**
  a split by index *plus* a cut per access *plus* a comparison of only the touched
  word (section 4.4): D4 compares whole tables, so its red at T of 64 or more (one
  access, 94,699 to 104,487 conflicts, every seed rejected by the default checker
  limits) says nothing about that combination. The limit is the checker, not memory:
  a million-conflict proof is 0.7 to 1.2 GB resident here, a proof of about 83,000
  conflicts is the smallest rejected for work, and a 457,844-conflict proof was
  accepted, so conflicts are not monotone in checker work.
* **One solver over many segments is the wrong form.** The incremental mode (one
  solver, assumptions per segment) took about two minutes for the 1,000
  per-instruction segments that certified mode (a fresh CNF each) did in about
  three seconds: the learned-clause database of one long incremental run slows every
  later segment (answering open question 4 of section 11.1).
* **Proxy.** Everything above is synthetic code with synthetic divergence (section
  8.4); VOS-029a's re-run of tiers B and D on the real compiled `pick`, `insert`
  and `remove` is what would turn proxy numbers into evidence.

**Consequence (section 8.3, row 1) and the expected path.** Do not fund VOS-029 as
planned *until the re-plan row VOS-028c has run*: cut density as a route invariant
(about 50,000 conflicts per obligation, below the checker's rejection at about
83,000, with a checker-work bound beside it), where cuts come from on compiled code
(SAT sweeping on B2, else the owned-compile lane becomes mandatory), memory as a
split plus per-access cuts plus touched-word comparison (a new D5), and two-safety
replaced by code-against-code trace shapes (backlog VOS-D096). VOS-029a's first
target (`fp-bitmap` `pick`, `insert`, `remove`: the A1 and A2 shapes) is green, so
**VOS-028c is expected to re-fund VOS-029a unchanged**. The drat-check limits are
not raised. The owner decides.

What this means for components being built meanwhile (VOS-010 and the `emu-core`
toys, VOS00 section 14.3 D4):

* Keep **N = 8** for X1: 65,536 cases per claim, 16.4% of the budget (VOS01 section
  5.6). The hard limits are N at most 10 for an operation with a full `U8` index and
  N at most 18 for state-only operations; **measured**: the generator accepts
  `insert` at N = 10 (262,144 cases, enumerated here) and refuses it at N = 11 with
  W0706 (524,288 cases).
* **Enumeration throughput** (host-specific, on the recording host): the real
  scheduler's committed harness runs 65,536 cases in 0.6 to 0.8 s (roughly 100,000
  cases per second, the figure of VOS02 section 13.2); the fixture's simpler harness
  runs 1.5 to 3 million per second. Time never bites; the 400,000-case budget does.
  The 64 MiB domain-byte bound never bites first for `Bytes<W>` binders (the case
  product passes 400,000 before the bytes pass 64 MiB; the widest single binder,
  `Bytes<4096>`, is 50.7 MB).
* X3 for bitmap state is width-insensitive (A1 and A2 are green to N = 64), so the
  X3 instance need not equal the X1 instance (section 5.1).
* For table-indexed state (a thread table), plan on T at most 16 per obligation, or
  one obligation per index and a cut between accesses, and keep a word from being
  updated twice in one obligation (the D2 and D3 cliff when k exceeds T).

### 8.6 The re-plan (VOS-028c)

**Status: specification, pre-registered before any run.** Section 8.5 left the
decision at row 1 with a supported reading narrower than its name: a *cut-density*
constraint. VOS-028c re-plans on that reading in three pull requests: VOS-028c1
(this section and the backlog, docs only), VOS-028c2 (the probe's budgets, `merge
--add` and hardening, no new experiment; section 9.12) and VOS-028c3 (the new
experiments, one recorded run and the decision below). Everything marked *planned*
is not built; the generated table of section 9.8 and the decision of section 8.5 are
untouched by this section. **The decision rule below is fixed here, before any new
row exists**: a change to a number, a knob or a row of the table after the results
are seen is a change to this specification and a re-run, never a tuning.

**What this section does not change.** The thresholds of 8.3 stay for every
experiment already recorded. The `drat-check` limits are never raised (section 6.3).
No row of the recorded file is edited by hand.

#### 8.6.1 The route budget

An obligation is **route-green** iff it is green under 8.3 **and** it needs at most
**50,000 conflicts** (8.3 allows 100,000) **and** `check_work` is at most **2^31**
(half of `DEFAULT_MAX_WORK`, 2^32). It is a stricter predicate than green, computed
from the same recorded fields (`worst_step_conflicts` for a chain or split, else
`conflicts`; `check_work`, which for a chain is the maximum over its obligations),
never typed. **Pre-registration honesty.** These two constants (50,000 conflicts, 2^31 check work)
were **derived from the recorded 1,625-row file before any VOS-028c run**, by the
measurement below. They are **stricter than the existing 8.3 thresholds** (100,000
conflicts, 2^32). They are now **frozen**: any change after a VOS-028c3 run is a
specification change with a re-run and a decision-log entry, never a tuning.

The two bounds answer two different facts of the recorded file:

* Conflicts are not monotone in checker work: a 457,844-conflict proof was accepted
  and a proof of about 83,000 was the smallest rejected for work (section 8.5), so a
  conflict bound alone does not keep a proof inside the checker. The work bound
  stands beside it.
* **Measured evidence for the bound** (`/usr/bin/python3` over
  `data/vos30-capacity-results.json`, 1,625 rows, recomputed for this section): of the
  **559** rows with a recorded `check_work` whose worst obligation is at most 50,000
  conflicts (540 UNSAT certified rows and 19 checked `collapsed` rows), every one is
  accepted by the default checker, and the largest `check_work` is **1,253,093,947**
  (0.58 of 2^31). Every one of the 24 rows the checker rejected has a worst obligation
  of at least 311,668 conflicts. Accepted rows whose *row-level* conflicts are
  30,000 to 82,835 reach `check_work` 2,514,237,761, above 2^31: that is why the cap
  is 50,000 conflicts and not 82,000. A bound of 2^31 is supported with a factor of
  1.7 of margin over the largest accepted row at or below the conflict cap.
  The 86 rows at or below 50,000 conflicts that ended `unknown` ended on the 120 s
  wall (VOS-D095), not on a verdict; they are not evidence either way.

The budget is a property of an *obligation*, so it is also a design rule: an
obligation must not be able to exceed it by construction. That is the next item.

#### 8.6.2 Cut density K

The cliff of 8.5 is chain depth: an obligation that chains more than about two to four
non-structurally-matched 32-bit operations exceeds the budget, and a cut after every
instruction is green by a factor of 60 or more. Define the **cut density K** as the
largest spacing in {1, 2, 4} instructions at which B3 in certified mode is route-green
at every length (25 to 1,000) and every divergence (5%, 25%, 100%) of the recorded
grid; K is 0 (undefined) if even spacing 1 is not. **K is the route invariant:** no
obligation spans more than K instructions of unmatched arithmetic. The recorded
grid jumps from spacing 1 to 8, so spacings 2 and 4 are *planned* (VOS-028c3 adds the
knobs `every2` and `every4` to B3 and records them, with the new experiments; VOS-028c2 adds no experiment). K is an output of the
measurement, not a choice: a larger K is cheaper to certify (fewer obligations) but is
only claimed where the rows say it is route-green.

#### 8.6.3 The experiments (registered, VOS-028c3c commit A)

All run with five seeds, in certified mode where a mode applies, judged by the
route-green predicate. No row is edited by hand. The new rows and the recorded ones
enter the committed file together, by the wholesale re-record on CI runners of
section 9.12.1 (pre-registered by VOS-028c3c-1), **not** by `merge --add`: the
committed file is tool 0.1.0 and its `tool` block records no tree ids, so `check --full`
refuses it and `merge --add` could never make it pass. The re-record replaces every recorded row,
so timings change and the verdicts of wall-limited rows may change with the host
(section 9.12.1 says what is kept and what is discarded).

* **B3 additions** (fixes K). `cut` gains `every2` and `every4` over the existing 6 x 3
  grid (lengths 25 to 1,000, divergence 5%, 25%, 100%), both modes: 360 rows, none in
  the quick tier.
* **D5, memory as split plus cut plus touched word** (section 4.4; the combination 8.5
  records as not measured at all). T in {4, 16, 64, 256} words, k in {1, 4, 16}
  accesses, split in {`index`, `none`}: 24 knobs, 120 rows; the quick knob is T = 4,
  k = 1. With `split = index`, one obligation per (index, access), T * k of them: the
  index is concrete, the pre-state is equated on the touched word only, and the written
  word is compared. With `split = none`, k obligations with a symbolic index. The
  **frame** (every other word unchanged) is a separate structural obligation per
  section 3.4: it is expected to be `collapsed` with reason `frame-untouched` or
  `propagation-only` (section 4.5), is reported in its own metrics and is excluded
  from the capacity count; if it needs conflicts it is an ordinary obligation. Its
  negative control (G3) needs **two mutants, both SAT and both replayed**: a wrong
  index or dropped store (which the touched-word obligation catches) and a stray extra
  store to another word (which only the frame obligation catches). The row is ok only
  if both are caught. T = 256 with k = 16 is 4,096 obligations, above the roughly 1,000
  of the feasibility count of 8.3; the row reports it and the memory verdict below
  counts obligations at their own number, it does not hide them.
* **B5, equivalence sweeping, a research experiment** (the route's escape hatch of
  VOS00 section 12 row 9). Instance length in {25 (quick), 200, 1,000}, divergence in
  {25%, 100%}, `fuse` in {1, 2, 4, 8}: 24 knobs, 120 rows; the quick knobs are length
  25 at `fuse = 1` (both divergences), the smallest, since the quick tier fails on an
  `unknown` row (VOS-D118). B2's divergence is a
  per-instruction alternative encoding, so every instruction's output word is already
  an equivalence point and sweeping B2 would rediscover B3 `every1` (a tautology, not a
  test). The `fuse` knob makes the points sparser. `fuse = w` starts from B2's program P
  (`gen_b` at the same length, divergence and seed) and turns every accumulating
  register-register `add a, a, b` into a run of w adds into `a`: the original followed by
  w - 1 adds `a += c_j`, each `c_j` an accumulator other than `a` drawn from a separate
  seeded stream, so P's own stream is untouched; the result is truncated to the instance
  length. The run's first add keeps P's alternative encoding; the reference computes the
  run right-nested, `a + (b + (c_2 + ... + c_w))`, so the run's intermediate values have
  no counterpart and equivalence points are at most w instructions apart (exactly w
  across each run; instructions outside runs stay per-instruction points). With
  `fuse = 1` there are no runs and the program, its alternative encodings and the
  instance are B2's (byte-identical DIMACS). **`fuse = 1` is B2**, and its
  `instance_sha256` must equal B2's (a cross-rule of `validate`). The sweep is
  deterministic: one AIG for both sides; 256 seeded simulation patterns (`Aig::simulate`) give candidate classes up to
  complement, the representative is the lowest node; candidates are proved in
  topological order, each lemma a fresh CNF over its cone with already-merged nodes
  substituted, cut at an 8-level frontier whose nodes are shared free inputs. A SAT
  answer at the frontier is *possibly spurious*: the depth doubles up to the full cone.
  Only a full-cone SAT is a real counterexample, and it refines the classes. Each UNSAT
  lemma is DRAT-checked under the default limits. Outputs that do not merge form one
  final obligation. Metrics added to a row: lemmas, candidates, refuted, escalations.
  **B5 is green iff every lemma and the final obligation are route-green and the total
  is under 600 s.** "The total" is the sweep's whole elapsed time, recorded as the
  row metric `sweep_ms`: from the start of the instance build through the sweep's verdict
  (simulation, AIG construction, every lemma solve and proof check, and the final obligation),
  with the G3 control excluded, because it is not part of the proof. It must be strictly
  under 600,000 ms, and `route_green` enforces it fail-closed: a B5 row with no finite,
  non-negative `sweep_ms` is not route-green. This definition was fixed before any recorded
  run (VOS-D118); the narrower sum `solve_ms` plus `check_ms` is not the total, since with
  an 1,800 s row wall it would let up to about 1,800 s of non-solver work pass. Lemma counts above 1,000
  per entry are reported and not gated; this is stated in advance. **B5's walls are
  measurement walls, not gates** (owner direction, 2026-11-23: relax timeouts where
  things need more time to prove out): the row wall is 1,800 s, each lemma solve and
  the final obligation get 600 s, and B5's G3 control gets its own 600 s and a
  1,000,000-conflict cap (the other experiments' controls keep 30 s and 300,000). They
  only decide whether a run produces an answer or ends `unknown`, so a slow sweep
  records its real conflicts; the route budget (50,000 conflicts, `check_work` 2^31),
  the 8.3 thresholds and the 600 s total are unchanged and judge the recorded numbers. G3: the mutated program through the same sweep must end
  SAT, its model checked against the **original unswept** control CNF and replayed on
  the simulator. Negative control: a sweeper that merges on simulation signature
  without proof is shown wrong on a mutant that agrees on all 256 patterns.

#### 8.6.4 The decision table

Evaluated **worst first; the first row whose condition holds is the outcome**; the table
is **total** (an experiment that is missing, a row that is not green, a guard failure
and anything else unclassified is red, as in 8.3). Pre-registered:

| Order | Outcome | Condition | Consequence |
|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | No lane | A1 or A2 (N = 8 to 64) or B3 `every1` at 200 instructions, 25% is not route-green | X2 plus the monitor is the ceiling; VOS-029a to c unfunded |
| R2 | Owned-compile lane mandatory | Not R1, and B5 is not route-green at 200 instructions, 25%, `fuse` = 2 | Fund VOS-029a unchanged (uses no cuts). VOS-029b waits on a source-map cut provider |
| R3 | Compiled lane, reduced | Not R1/R2, and B5 red at 1,000 instructions or at `fuse` = 4, or K = 1 | Fund VOS-029a and b, sweeping as cut source under stated length/density limits |
| R4 | Compiled lane | Otherwise | Fund VOS-029a and b |

A red B5 at `fuse` = 2 is a valid, expected-possible outcome (R2), not a failure of the
experiment: it means compiled code needs cuts by construction (the owned-compile lane
of VER13 section 4.3 item 1), and open question 6 of 11.1 becomes a backlog row.
B5 may also be red from lemma CNF size alone; that is reported as measured and not
tuned away (changing the frontier is a specification change and a re-run).

**Memory is an orthogonal verdict**, not a row of the table: if D5 is route-green at
T = 64 and T = 256 with k = 16, the `thread-table` X3 (VOS-029b, VOS-C02x) runs at its
shipped T; otherwise it runs at **T at most 16 per obligation** (section 8.5's
guidance stands).

**What VOS-029a hinges on.** Only on A1 and A2 route-green at every N and B3 `every1`
route-green at 200 instructions, 25% divergence: not R1. The recorded worst cases are
547 conflicts (A1 and A2) and 1,654 conflicts (B3 `every1`), both far inside 50,000,
so re-funding VOS-029a unchanged is *expected*; it stays provisional until a named
reviewer has regenerated the full tier (section 8.5). VOS-029a uses no cuts, so B5, D5
and K do not gate it; they gate VOS-029b and the memory shapes.

**Two-safety no longer gates VOS-029a.** Section 3.6 already says VOS-029 delivers no
two-safety on a real entry; C1 is dropped from the shapes required for funding and
the code-against-code trace shapes are their own backlog row (VOS-D116), outside this
re-plan. Why this is safe: VOS-029a delivers functional X3 only (section 3.6), so
dropping C1 from its gate weakens no claim it makes. Three rules keep it so. (1) **No
VOS document may claim a two-safety or side-channel property of VOS-029 output** until
VOS-D116 is done. (2) **VOS-D116 must be decided before any profile or theorem cites
such a property.** (3) This change of gate (8.3 row 1 to 8.6.4) requires the **named
reviewer's sign-off at the same time as the full-tier regeneration** of section 9.12
item (1); until then the funding of VOS-029a stays provisional.

**Re-plan decision (VOS-028c): R3, compiled lane, reduced** (row R3 of 8.6.4), with
**cut density K = 2** and the memory shapes **at their shipped T**. It is computed by the
library's `decide_replan` from the CI recording of VOS-028c3c (section 8.5: run 45486250172,
attempt 1, repository revision `1bb7682263e3`, 64 shards on GitHub `ubuntu-24.04`, `--jobs 2`,
joined file SHA-256 `85cde21d02dab3ce9fc40f26a5bd9bedc1c0a0fc29ad8ca8885ecc810799a453`).
**Provisional until the named reviewer signs (VOS-028c3d)**, as 8.6.4 requires; the 8.3
outcome (row 1) is unchanged. The label, K and the memory verdict are **machine-generated and
machine-checked** (VOS-D142, VOS-028c3c-3b-1c): `decide --replan` prints `decide_replan`'s answer
from the results file, `table --write` renders the same lines into the generated block that follows
(between the replan markers, a comment pair distinct from the 9.8 table's), and `table --check`
(a) compares that block byte for byte with a regeneration and (b) fails if this paragraph's label
(the row, its title, `K = n` and whether the memory shapes run at their shipped T) differs from
`decide_replan`'s, so a hand-edited label fails `check`. The check **fails closed** like the 9.8
block: with a results file, a spec with no `### 8.6 ` section, no replan block or no label is a
finding, the label must occur exactly once in 8.6, `K = n` must not be a prefix of a longer number,
each marker must stand alone on its line, and CRLF line ends are read as LF. The sentence above is hand-written prose
about the generated answer; only the marked block is generated.

<!-- vos30-replan:begin -->
Generated by `table --write` from the results file (`decide --replan` prints the same lines). Do not edit: `table --check` compares this block and the label above it with the rows.

* row R3: Compiled lane, reduced
* cut density K = 2
* memory: shipped T (D5 split=index route-green at T = 64 and T = 256, k = 16)
* because: B5 at 1,000 instructions, div = 25%, fuse = 1: NOT route-green; worst conflicts 0, worst check_work 0
* because: B5 at 1,000 instructions, div = 25%, fuse = 2: NOT route-green; worst conflicts 18137, worst check_work 0
* because: B5 at 1,000 instructions, div = 25%, fuse = 4: NOT route-green; worst conflicts 237, worst check_work 0
* because: B5 at 1,000 instructions, div = 25%, fuse = 8: NOT route-green; worst conflicts 288, worst check_work 0
* because: B5 at 1,000 instructions, div = 100%, fuse = 1: NOT route-green; worst conflicts 0, worst check_work 0
* because: B5 at 1,000 instructions, div = 100%, fuse = 2: NOT route-green; worst conflicts 19938, worst check_work 0
* because: B5 at 1,000 instructions, div = 100%, fuse = 4: NOT route-green; worst conflicts 237, worst check_work 0
* because: B5 at 1,000 instructions, div = 100%, fuse = 8: NOT route-green; worst conflicts 288, worst check_work 0
* because: B5 at 25 instructions, div = 25%, fuse = 4: NOT route-green; worst conflicts 345066, worst check_work 4251824668
* because: B5 at 25 instructions, div = 100%, fuse = 4: NOT route-green; worst conflicts 484691, worst check_work 4256395230
* because: B5 at 200 instructions, div = 25%, fuse = 4: NOT route-green; worst conflicts 479324, worst check_work 0
* because: B5 at 200 instructions, div = 100%, fuse = 4: NOT route-green; worst conflicts 479324, worst check_work 0
<!-- vos30-replan:end -->

* **Not R1.** A1 and A2 are route-green at every N from 8 to 64 (worst 547 conflicts) and B3
  `every1` is route-green at 200 instructions, 25% divergence (worst step 1,654 conflicts).
* **Not R2.** B5 at 200 instructions, 25% divergence, `fuse` = 2 is route-green: five seeds,
  all UNSAT, worst obligation 22,158 conflicts (cap 50,000), worst `check_work` 375,130,071
  (cap 2^31). B5 at `fuse` = 2 is also route-green at 200 instructions with 100% divergence
  (the same worst obligation and `check_work`) and at 25 instructions (both divergences), so
  sweeping works at these sizes when equivalence points are at most 2 instructions apart.
* **R3 because B5 is red at 1,000 instructions and at `fuse` = 4.** Per group of five seeds,
  as recorded (route-green means every seed is green, at most 50,000 conflicts per
  obligation, `check_work` at most 2^31 and a total under 600 s):
  * *1,000 instructions, `fuse` = 1 (both divergences):* all five seeds `killed`, cap `wall`,
    0 conflicts and no solve time: the 1,800 s row wall killed a stage that did not return,
    and no verdict was recorded (note on the rows: "the wall-clock kill ended a stage that
    did not return").
  * *1,000 instructions, `fuse` = 2:* all five seeds `unknown`, cap `wall`, 11.6 to 15.8
    million conflicts in 1,248 to 2,064 s of solving; the worst single obligation is 18,137
    conflicts at 25% and 19,938 at 100%, inside the 50,000 budget, so it is the wall (the
    600 s total) that fails the row.
  * *1,000 instructions, `fuse` = 4 and 8:* all five seeds `unknown`, cap `wall`, about 4.3
    to 8.2 million conflicts in about 567 to 588 s; worst single obligation 237 conflicts
    (`fuse` = 4) and 288 (`fuse` = 8), for both divergences.
  * *25 instructions, `fuse` = 4:* every seed exceeds the budget: worst obligation 345,066
    conflicts (25%) and 484,691 (100%). At 25% all five seeds are UNSAT with `check_work` of
    2.2 to 4.3 billion, every one above the route bound of 2^31 and none above the checker's
    default limit of 2^32; at 100% four seeds are UNSAT with `check_work` of 3.5 to 4.3
    billion (all four above 2^31, none above 2^32) and one is `unknown`, cap `wall`.
  * *200 instructions, `fuse` = 4:* all ten seeds `unknown`, cap `wall` at about 291 to 310
    s, with 479,324 as the worst single obligation at both divergences, after 10.9 to
    17.9 million conflicts; no proof was produced.
  `fuse` = 8 is also red at 25 and 200 instructions (all seeds `unknown`, cap `conflicts`,
  worst obligation up to 988,289 conflicts) but is not in the R3 condition.
* **K = 2.** B3 certified with a cut every 2 instructions is route-green in all 18 groups
  (lengths 25 to 1,000, divergence 5%, 25%, 100%): worst step 21,929 conflicts, worst
  `check_work` 76,608,495. A cut every 4 is not: four seeds, all at 100% divergence, exceed the 50,000
  budget: 70,305 conflicts at 200 instructions, 67,894 at 500 and 1,000, and 53,014 at 1,000.
  Spacing 1 stays the safe floor (worst step 1,654).
* **Memory: shipped T.** D5 with `split = index` is route-green at T = 64 and T = 256 with
  k = 16 (every seed UNSAT, worst obligation 4,188 conflicts, worst `check_work`
  12,874,426), so the `thread-table` X3 (VOS-029b, VOS-C02x) runs at its shipped T and the
  "T at most 16" fallback does not apply.

**What R3 funds, and the limits it states.** VOS-029a and VOS-029b are funded; sweeping is
the cut source for compiled code only inside what the recording shows route-green: at most
200 instructions per swept entry and equivalence points at most 2 instructions apart
(`fuse` at most 2, matching K = 2). A compiled entry whose equivalence points are 4 or more
instructions apart, or that is 1,000 instructions long, is **not** covered by B5; it needs
cuts from another source (the owned-compile lane of VER13 section 4.3 item 1, where a source
map gives cut points by construction) or a shorter decomposition. Disclosure: the risk that
`fuse` = 4 would not be route-green was recorded in the decision log (2026-11-23) before this
recording; the recording confirms it and nothing was tuned. B5's `fuse` is a generator knob,
a proxy for compiled-code equivalence points (8.4): whether real compiled code has points at
most 2 apart is what VOS-029b's re-run on the real object tests.

**Proxy.** Every shape above is synthetic code with a synthetic divergence (section
8.4); B5 in addition measures a proxy for compiled-code equivalence points (`fuse`
is a knob of the generator, not something a compiler does).
