## 9. The probe (VOS-028b)

VOS-028b implements this section without redesign. Everything a reader needs to
build and run it is stated here: the instance generator, the experiments, the
guards, the measurements, the budget, the result file, the table and its check.

### 9.1 Where it lives, and what it is

* Program: `code/programs/rust/vos-capacity-probe`, class P in `vos.toml`
  (VOS03 section 2). Not a library; no other crate depends on it.
* Dependencies: path dependencies only: `bit-blaster`, `cdcl-sat`, `drat-check`,
  `riscv-simulator`, `riscv-encoder`, plus (VOS-028b) `coding_adventures_sha256`
  (digests), `coding_adventures_bounded_json` (the results file),
  `coding_adventures_warrant_grammar` (E2 and the N = 11 refusal), `vos-sched-fp-bitmap`
  with its `ghost` feature, `vos-sched-contract`, `vos-probe-ready` and
  `coding_adventures_test_bootstrap` (what the included generated harnesses use).
  **Zero third-party crates.**
* Fixtures (decided in VOS-028b, because VOS-011 has landed): the **real production
  scheduler `vos-sched-fp-bitmap`** is the fixture of every E1 knob that fits N = 8
  (binder products 256 and 65,536), through its *committed generated harness*
  (`tests/generated/warrant_evidence.rs`), included into the probe with `include!`
  and driven in process through the harness's own public `exh_<claim>(Fault)` entry
  points (the real crate's test target is `harness = false` and refuses a filtered
  run that is not a sabotage run, so a per-claim run-only rate cannot be taken from
  its binary; the in-process route needs no change to a production crate). The
  real crate is fixed at N = 8, so the knobs it cannot give (`insert` at N = 10,
  the 393,216-case binder product, the N = 11 refusal, the `Impl(mask: U8)` form at
  a second width) come from the **committed fixture package `vos-probe-ready`**
  (`code/packages/rust/vos-probe-ready`, class H, with its `.warrant`,
  `evidence.bindings` and generated harness at the fixed generator paths): the
  VOS01 section 5 toy, kept and narrowed to exactly what the real component cannot
  provide. It is no longer "replaced": it never claims to be the scheduler.
* Results: `code/specs/data/vos30-capacity-results.json`, written only by the
  tool, and the generated table of section 9.8.

**Instance generation: a probe-private minimal symbolic RV32I stepper** (a few
hundred lines, `cpu.rs`), over hand-assembled instruction sequences:

* built on the `bit-blaster` (`Aig`, `bv`, `Tseitin`);
* sequences assembled with `riscv-encoder` and **decoded with
  `riscv-simulator::decode`** (so the probe consumes the same decoder as the
  future executor and cannot disagree about encodings);
* covers the RV32I ALU operations, `lw`/`sw`/`lbu`/`sb`, and forward branches
  with a concrete PC.

It is **not** VOS-029's executor and is not in any trusted base. Alternatives
rejected, with the reason, so they are not reopened:

| Alternative | Why not |
|---|---|
| Real `rustc` RV32 output | No `riscv32` rustup target is installed, and VOS22 (hermetic image build) does not exist |
| WIR `sym.rs` formulas as the instance | The wrong proxy: they refuse `CallFunction`, use 64-bit offsets with per-allocation provenance, and are exactly the miters the audit found collapsing |
| Reuse the future executor | It does not exist; the probe is meant to run first and decide whether the executor is funded |

### 9.2 Entry points

```
vos-capacity-probe list
vos-capacity-probe run --tier quick|full [--only A1,B3] [--jobs N] [--seeds N] --out results.json
vos-capacity-probe check [--full] results.json [--jobs N] [--exe PATH]
vos-capacity-probe run --tier full [--jobs N] [--seeds N] --shard K/N [--run-id DIGITS] [--run-attempt DIGITS] --out shard.json
vos-capacity-probe check --full --shard K/N [--rerun-out F] [--run-id DIGITS --run-attempt DIGITS] results.json [--jobs N] [--exe PATH]
vos-capacity-probe decision-diff --recorded R.json --rerun J.json [--expect-shards N]
vos-capacity-probe join --out J [--expect-binary-sha256 H] [--expect-run-id I] [--expect-run-attempt A] [--expect-shards N] [--expect-jobs J] SHARD.json...
vos-capacity-probe merge [--add] --base A.json --fresh B.json --experiment E1 [--where field=value] [--ignore-outside] --out C.json
vos-capacity-probe table [--check | --write] [--results FILE] [--spec FILE]
vos-capacity-probe decide [--results FILE]
vos-capacity-probe row --spec JSON --wall SECONDS          (the child process of run)
```

Exit status 0 is success, 1 means the command ran and found violations, 2 means a
malformed command line or an unreadable input (a gate never confuses the two).

* `list` prints each experiment, its knob counts per tier and its guards.
* `run` executes the selected experiments, **one child process per row** so peak
  resident memory is attributable (each child is wrapped with `/usr/bin/time -l`
  on macOS or `-v` on Linux; the tool parses it), and records one row per
  `(experiment, knob, seed)`. At least five seeds per knob (`--seeds` may raise
  it, never lower it). `--tier quick` also applies the BUILD gate of section 9.3.
* `check` validates a results file against the schema and the guards, **re-runs
  every quick-tier row** and compares digests, verdicts, controls and conflict
  counts, requires that the file contain every quick row of the current plan, and
  exits 1 on any violation; full-tier rows are validated for schema and guards
  and then **spot-checked** (a seeded sample re-run in child processes, 9.12 item 1)
  and are regenerated by a reviewer (section 9.6). **As built (VOS-028c2):** the
  validation also holds every row's `(experiment, mode, knob)` to the plan's closed
  set, values included; `check` prints a non-fatal `stale:` line per recorded input id
  that differs from the working tree and the line `spot check: population P, re-run
  R, skipped (plan cost class expensive): S, not reached: N`; **`check --full`** refuses unless every
  recorded tree id equals the working tree's (a file that records none refuses too)
  and then re-runs every full-tier row in a child process; `--exe` names the
  executable the children run (a test passes it).
* `merge` is the only sanctioned way to refresh a SUBSET of the committed rows (the file is never
  edited by hand): it replaces the base rows of one experiment (optionally those whose knob string
  field equals a value, `--where fixture=vos-sched-fp-bitmap`) by the same-identity rows of a fresh
  `run`, leaves every other row byte-identical and in place, and records the refresh.
  **Provenance is per row, so the base `tool`, `host` and `revisions` are kept** as the file's
  original provenance and the merge appends one entry to a top-level `merges` list (omitted while
  empty): `{ "rev", "binary_sha256", "dirty_tree", "experiment", "where", "rows": [row ids], "at" }`,
  with `rev` the fresh run's `repo` revision. `validate` checks every entry (a 40-hex `rev`, a
  64-hex binary digest, `dirty_tree` false, a known experiment, every listed row id present in the
  file, no id listed twice), and the table banner says "N rows refreshed at rev X".
  `merge` refuses, writing nothing: a fresh run for which `validate` reports anything (`validate`
  applies unchanged to a partial run: it needs only non-empty rows, five seeds per knob and
  the row rules); a merged result for which `validate` reports anything; a `dirty_tree` that is not
  the JSON boolean `false` (in `merge` and in `validate`: a string or `null` never reads as clean);
  a fresh `repo` revision that is not HEAD or an ancestor of it (`git merge-base --is-ancestor`);
  duplicate row ids in either file; fresh rows outside the selected subset (unless `--ignore-outside`
  says to drop them, as when `run --only E1` re-ran fixtures that are not being refreshed);
  a `--where` field that no base row of the experiment has; a subset whose identities
  `(experiment, knob, mode, seed)` differ between the two files (it never adds, drops or renames a
  row). Reads are capped at 64 MiB and the output is written to a temporary file next to the target
  and renamed over it. Refresh procedure, for example after a change to `vos-sched-fp-bitmap`'s
  generated document (VOS-D103): commit; `run --tier full --only E1 --out F`; `merge --base R
  --fresh F --experiment E1 --where fixture=vos-sched-fp-bitmap --ignore-outside --out R`;
  `table --write`; commit the results file (a second commit, so the tree that ran was clean).
  **`merge --add` (as built, VOS-028c2).** The rule above that a merge never adds a row
  cannot take a new experiment knob: the identities of the base and the fresh file
  differ, and `check` requires every quick row of the plan to be present, so a plan
  change and its rows must land in the same PR. `merge --add` accepts only fresh rows
  whose identities `(experiment, knob, mode, seed)` are all **absent** from the base,
  appends them, and records `"kind": "add"` in the `merges` entry (the key is omitted
  for a refresh, so the existing entry keeps its bytes). It refuses any overlap with the
  base, and every other refusal above applies unchanged **except the two that only a
  refresh can meet**: that the subset's identities equal the base's, and that a
  `--where` field exists in a base row of the experiment (a new experiment has no base
  row). It also refuses an empty subset. The new rows are appended after the base
  rows, in file order, and every base row keeps its bytes. New rows are then recorded
  as `run --tier full --only B3,B5,D5 --out F`, then `merge --add --base R --fresh F
  --experiment B3 --ignore-outside --out R` (and B5, D5), then `table --write`.
  **Superseded for the VOS-028c3c recording (VOS-028c3c-1):** that procedure is how a
  subset is added on one host, and it is not how the re-plan is recorded. The
  recording is a wholesale re-record of every row on CI runners, joined from shards
  (section 9.12.1); `merge --add` stays for a later, smaller addition.
* `table` renders the markdown block of section 9.8 from the committed results
  file; `--check` fails if the VOS30 spec's generated block differs, and
  `--write` splices the block between the markers.
* `decide` prints the section 8.3 outcome and the conditions that placed it.

**As built (VOS-028c3c-2b-i): `run --shard`, the `recording` block and `join`.**
`check --full --shard`, `--rerun-out` and `decision-diff` are VOS-028c3c-2b-ii (below).

* **`run --shard K/N`** requires `--tier full` and refuses `--only` (a shard is a slice
  of the whole plan). `K` and `N` are decimal integers with `0 <= K < N` and
  `1 <= N <= 1024`. It runs `exp::plan_shard(K, N)`: the knobs of the plan in plan order
  (`exp::full_knobs`, the knobs of every experiment in `EXPERIMENTS` order), knob `i`
  to shard `i mod N`, every seed of a knob in the same shard. The rows keep plan order.
* **The `recording` block.** A new optional top-level key, written after `regeneration`
  and omitted when absent (so the schema number stays 2 and the committed file keeps its
  bytes): `{ "mode": "record", "run_id": "...", "run_attempt": "...", "shard": "K/N",
  "jobs": J }`. `run_id` and `run_attempt` come only from the explicit `--run-id` and
  `--run-attempt` flags, each ASCII digits, 1 to 20 of them, never from the
  environment. A shard run with neither flag records the block without them (both keys
  omitted); giving one flag without the other is refused. `mode` is `record` for `run`
  and `verify` for `check --full --shard --rerun-out` (2b-ii, below). On the joined file the block is
  `{ "mode", "run_id", "run_attempt", "shards": N, "jobs": J }`: `shard` becomes `shards`.
* **`join --out J FILE...`** refuses, with status 2 and **writing nothing** (the output
  is built in memory, then written to a temporary file and renamed), unless: every input
  is in canonical form and has no structural `validate` finding; every input records a
  `shard` and the set of shards is exactly `0..N` with one `N` throughout; the tool name
  and version, the 64-hex `binary_sha256`, the (non-empty) `trees`, the `revisions`, the
  host `triple` (not `os`: VOS-D133), the recording mode, `jobs`, `run_id` and
  `run_attempt` are identical in every input; no input has a `merges` entry or a
  `regeneration` banner; and each input's row identities equal `plan_shard(K, N)` exactly
  (which gives disjointness and union = plan in one comparison). Inputs that record no
  run id (a local run) must all lack it, and then any `--expect-run-id` or
  `--expect-run-attempt` is refused. `--expect-binary-sha256`, `--expect-run-id`,
  `--expect-run-attempt`, `--expect-shards` and `--expect-jobs` each refuse on a
  mismatch (section 9.12.2, items 1 and 2; VOS-D127 L1). The output holds the rows in
  plan order and the `recording` block above. Row-class findings of the joined file are
  printed as `join: finding: ...` on stderr and do **not** refuse (they are kept row
  outcomes, section 9.12.1). The join of a quick-tier or `--only` run is refused by the
  plan comparison.

**As built (VOS-028c3c-2b-ii): the verify fan-in, VOS-D132 and VOS-D134.**

* **`check --full --shard K/N [--rerun-out F] [--run-id I --run-attempt A] FILE`.**
  `--shard` without `--full` is refused (status 2), as are `--rerun-out`, `--run-id` and
  `--run-attempt` without `--shard`, `--run-id` without `--run-attempt` (or the reverse),
  and `--run-id` without `--rerun-out`; every flag goes through the same reader as `run`
  and `join` (a repeated flag is refused). All of this is decided before the file is read.
  The WHOLE file is then validated exactly as `check --full` does today (the guards below,
  `validate`, quick and full plan coverage, `full_refusals`), and only then are the rows
  whose identities are in `plan_shard(5, K, N)` re-run, quick tier in process and full
  tier in children, with the comparison classes of 9.12.1 (`compare_rerun` and
  `compare_timing`). A shard that holds no quick-tier row is not a finding (the whole-file
  run still says "the file has no quick-tier row to re-run"). The decision comparison
  runs over the recorded rows with the shard's re-run rows laid over them. Exit status:
  1 on any difference (a row finding), 2 on a usage or structural refusal.
* **`--rerun-out F`** writes the shard's RE-RUN rows (in the recorded file's order, `green`
  recomputed; a quick row borrows its recorded resident set, as in `check --full`) as a
  canonical results file whose `meta` is the VERIFYING binary's own (`meta::gather`:
  binary digest, trees, revisions, triple) with `merges` and `regeneration` empty and a
  `recording` of `{ "mode": "verify", "shard": "K/N", "jobs": J }` (plus `run_id` and
  `run_attempt` when given). It is never overwritten: an existing `F` is refused BEFORE
  any re-run, and the write is atomic and publish-if-absent (a hard link, which fails if `F`
  appeared in the meantime; `join --out` publishes the same way). `--exe`, if given with
  `--rerun-out`, must be the running binary, whose digest the `meta` records; a `--shard` slice with
  no row of the plan is refused, and a finding is raised if the rows re-run differ from the slice's
  rows in the file. It is written before the verdict, so a shard with
  differences still leaves its file for the fan-in. `join` takes verify shards exactly as
  it takes record shards (the mode is one of `record`, `verify`, identical across the
  inputs) and its output carries `mode: "verify"` and `shards: N`.
* **`decision-diff --recorded R --rerun J [--expect-shards N]`.** Reads both files (canonical,
  capped) and AUTHENTICATES the re-run before comparing a row (all status 2): `J`'s `recording` is
  mode `verify` and joined (`shards` set, no `shard`), `R`'s is not mode `verify`, the two record
  equal non-empty tree ids, `check_guards` is empty for both, and `--expect-shards`, when given,
  equals `J`'s `shards`; so a file compared with itself, or with any file that merely holds the
  same rows, is not a verification. Then it requires the two row-identity sets to be equal and each free of a repeated identity
  (else status 2: `J` is not a re-run of `R`), orders `J`'s rows as `R`'s, and runs
  `results::decision_differences`; it solves and re-runs nothing. Status 0 and one
  `decision-diff: ok` line when there is no difference, status 1 with one
  `decision-diff:` line per difference otherwise. The fan-in is therefore: N
  `check --full --shard K/N --rerun-out`, one `join` of the N verify files, one
  `decision-diff` of the recorded file against the joined one.
* **VOS-D132: `decision_differences` compares more than `decide`.** Besides the section
  8.3 row and its time-stripped `because` lines, it compares `cut_density_k`, the
  `decide_replan` row (R1 to R4), the re-plan memory verdict (`memory_shipped_t`) and the
  re-plan `because` lines with the counters cut (each line ends `; worst conflicts C,
  worst check_work W`, which a wall-capped row varies between runs; the row name and its
  route-green verdict stay). Before this a verification could pass while the re-plan
  outcome or `K` differed. Its only caller outside the tests is `check --full`.
* **VOS-D134: guards before any re-run, in every `check` mode.** `results::check_guards`
  returns (a) every structural finding of `validate_classes`, (b) any row identity
  `(experiment, mode, knob, seed)` that appears twice, and (c) a count of quick-tier
  specs above the plan's quick-row count (85; 105 since VOS-028c3c commit A). Any of them is printed as `check:
  structural: ...` and `check` exits **2** (`Failure::Usage`: "nothing was re-run"), the
  class-1 outcome of 9.12.1; before, a structural finding was one of the exit-1
  violations and (b) and (c) did not exist. Plan coverage and the row findings keep
  exit 1. **As built (VOS-028c3c-2c): the overall budget.** Plain `check` (not
  `--full`, whose rows are re-run under their own walls and shard clocks) re-runs its
  quick-tier rows under `results::QUICK_RERUN_BUDGET`, **1,800 s in all, checked between
  rows, so the worst case is the budget plus one row's wall**. Before a worker
  takes the next row it compares the time elapsed since the re-run began with the budget;
  once it is spent no further row starts and `check` reports one finding, `the quick-tier
  re-run exceeded its overall budget of 1800 s: K of N rows were re-run and the rest were not started`, and exits 1, so a
  crafted file cannot make BUILD spend N times a row wall. A row already running is
  bounded by its own wall (`rerun_wall`) and is not interrupted. The constant is
  generous on purpose: the 85 committed quick rows reproduce in about 10 s on a Windows
  developer machine at `--jobs 4` (measured, including the cargo start), so the budget
  is more than 150 times what an honest file needs and bites only a file built to be slow.
* **`sign` (VOS-028c3c-2c, VOS-D120).** `sign FILE --reviewer NAME --host LABEL --run-id I
  --run-attempt A --head-sha S --expect-sha256 H --verify-file J --expect-verify-sha256 V
  --out OUT` writes the `regeneration` banner (the last two flags: amended by VOS-028c3c-3b-1b,
  VOS-D141, below). It writes a NEW file with the same publish-if-absent write as `join --out`
  and never edits `FILE` in place (the file is evidence, and an overwritten recording is
  worse than a refused signature; the reviewer renames `OUT` over `FILE` in the PR). Every
  refusal is status 2 and writes nothing. It refuses unless: `FILE` is canonical; its
  banner is null and its `merges` are empty; its `recording` is mode `record`, JOINED (`shards` set, no `shard`),
  and carries a run id and attempt; `--run-id` and `--run-attempt` equal that block's;
  `--head-sha` is 40 hex digits, equals `revisions.repo`, and names a commit whose input
  ids equal the recorded `trees` (VOS-D137: `git rev-parse <head_sha>:<path>` for each of
  the input entries of section 9.2 `trees`, run without a shell, the id read back and checked to be 40 hex digits;
  not an ancestry test, because main is squash-merged and the commit a CI recording ran at is
  never in main's history after the merge, so an ancestry rule would refuse every signature
  at main; a refusal naming `git fetch origin <sha>` if the commit is not in the reviewer's
  object store, since GitHub keeps a PR's head commits; a refusal if git cannot say);
  `--reviewer` and `--host`
  pass the charset rule below; `missing_tree_refusals` is empty (amended by VOS-028c3c-3b-1b: the
  recorded `trees` are present and well formed and the checkout has no uncommitted input, but they
  no longer have to equal the working tree's, because the verify file below is what vouches for the
  working tree; the stale half of `full_refusals` is the only check dropped); `validate`'s
  structural class is empty; the full plan is covered; the verify evidence below holds; and
  `--expect-sha256`, which is REQUIRED (a usage error when absent), equals the SHA-256
  of the bytes of `FILE`: the reviewer passes the joined file's SHA-256 that the join job
  printed, so the sign-off is tied to the file the join job produced and not to whatever
  file sits in the reviewer's checkout. Row findings do not refuse: they are kept row
  outcomes (9.12.1).
  **The verify evidence (VOS-028c3c-3b-1b, VOS-D141).** `--verify-file J` and
  `--expect-verify-sha256 V` are both REQUIRED (a usage error when absent). `J` is the joined verify
  file of a dispatched `check --full --at-checkout` run, and `sign` refuses unless all of these
  hold: `J` is read once and the SHA-256 of those very bytes equals `V` (64 hex digits, case not
  significant); `J` is canonical, its `recording` is mode `verify` and JOINED (`shards` set, no
  `shard`) with a run id and attempt, and its `shards` and `jobs` equal the recorded file's; `J` has
  no structural finding (the findings `check` stops on) and no merges; `J`'s `trees` pass the
  committed-tree rule (exactly the entries of 9.2, hex object ids, none `+uncommitted`) AND equal
  the working tree's ids, so the verify vouches for the tree the reviewer has checked out;
  `J.recording.verifies_sha256` equals the unsigned SHA-256 of `FILE` (so `J` verified this very
  file, and the digest is the same before and after the banner); `J` and `FILE` hold the same row
  identities; every identity is clean under `results::compare_verify`; and
  `results::decision_differences` is empty. The last three are the comparison `decision-diff` made in
  the workflow, re-made here by the same function over the same data, so the evidence does not rest
  on the workflow's exit code alone. A tree difference between `FILE` and the working tree is not a
  refusal: that is the case a verify at the checkout exists for.
  **The banner** is `{ "reviewer", "host", "run_id", "run_attempt", "head_sha",
  "unsigned_sha256", "verified": { "run_id", "run_attempt", "head_sha", "verify_sha256",
  "trees" } }`, canonical JSON, and `null` while absent, so the committed
  file keeps its bytes. `verified` is REQUIRED in any banner (no file was signed before it
  existed): `run_id` and `run_attempt` are the joined verify file's `recording` values, `head_sha`
  is its `revisions.repo`, `verify_sha256` is `V`, and `trees` is its `trees` (where the verify
  ran). `validate` checks its shape (digits, 40-hex revision, 64-hex digest, `trees` passing the
  committed-tree rule); it cannot recompute `verify_sha256`, because `J` is not in the file. The
  unsigned digest ignores the whole banner, `verified` included. `unsigned_sha256` is the SHA-256 of the bytes of the input file,
  which is the digest the join job prints (9.12.2 item 1, VOS-D127 I1); `validate` (and so
  `check`, `table` and `merge`) recomputes it by rendering the file again with the
  banner removed and reports, as a STRUCTURAL finding, a mismatch, a malformed field
  a banner on a file with `merges`, or a banner on a file whose `recording` is not a joined record: a row edited after signing
  is caught, and the digest the reviewer saw stays checkable. Signing changes no row byte
  and no other `meta` byte. There is no timestamp (`utc_now` shells out to `date`, which
  Windows lacks, and the run id dates the recording). `reviewer` and `host` are 1 to 64
  characters from a strict whitelist (ASCII letters, digits, space and `. _ , ' @ -`),
  with no leading, trailing or doubled space, because `table` splices the banner into this
  Markdown file and any other character can make a link, an image, emphasis, a heading,
  a table cell, a code span or HTML. `table` (`--check` and `--write` alike) also refuses,
  with status 2, a results file that has any structural finding, so a hand-edited banner
  never reaches this file. `reviewer` and `host` are self-reported and are not covered
  by `unsigned_sha256`: the banner is an unauthenticated attestation (9.6), and the
  control is the human process of 9.12.2 item 7. `merge` on a
  signed file clears the banner (a merged file is a new recording, and the old digest
  would no longer match). `join` still refuses a banner on a shard.

**The CI host, parallelism and sharding (pre-registered by VOS-028c3c-1; built
through 2b-ii).** `run --shard K/N`, `check --full --shard K/N`, `join` and
`decision-diff` are VOS-028c3c-2b; the workflow that runs them is VOS-028c3c-3. The values below are fixed
here, before any code or recording exists. A change to one after a recording is a
change to this specification and a re-record, never a tuning.

* **Host.** The recording and every full verify run on GitHub-hosted `ubuntu-24.04`
  standard runners of this public repository. We rely on the runner class's published
  limits and do not measure them: 4 vCPU, 16 GB of memory and a 6 h job limit (assumed;
  if GitHub changes them, this section is stale). **One binary is built once per
  workflow run** and passed to every shard by `--exe` (integrity: section 9.12.2). Record and verify use the same
  runner label, the same `--jobs` and the same N, because the host class changes
  timings (section 9.12.1).
* **`--jobs 2`, for record and verify.** The 4 vCPU are assumed to be 2 physical cores
  with SMT. The timing gates are absolute (the 10 s green threshold of 8.3 and B5's
  600 s route total of 8.6.3), so contention between children must be bounded, and two
  at a time keeps it so. Memory is not the constraint: `RSS_CAP_KB` is 2 GiB per row
  and the recorded peak is 1,112,352 kB (about 1,086 MiB), so two children use well
  under 16 GB.
* **Sharding.** `run --shard K/N` and `check --full --shard K/N` give knob `i` (0-based,
  in plan order) to shard `i mod N`; all seeds of a knob stay in one shard. **N = 64.**
  Plain round-robin suffices: the 24 B5 knobs are registered as one contiguous block of the plan, so with
  N at least 24 no shard holds more than one B5 knob (and at most one D5 knob and at
  most three B3 knobs). VOS-028c3c-2 pins "no shard holds more than one B5 knob" with a
  unit test.
* **Sizing, disclosed.** Row walls are 120 s, 600 s for the chains (B3, D3, D4, D5) and
  1,800 s for B5; the hard limits `runner::hard_limit_for` gives are 300 s, 780 s and
  2,550 s (wall + control stage + 150 s kill slack: 120 + 30 + 150, 600 + 30 + 150,
  1,800 + 600 + 150). After activation the plan is **445 knobs and 2,225 rows** (new:
  B3 `every2`/`every4` 72 knobs and 360 rows, D5 24 knobs and 120 rows, B5 24 knobs
  and 120 rows). The worst single knob is B5, 5 x 2,550 s = 3.54 h serial, about 2.13 h
  at `--jobs 2` (three rounds of two). The **absolute bound** (every solver row killed
  at its hard limit) gives a worst shard of 4.75 h at N = 64 and `--jobs 2`, and about
  220 runner-hours per full run. The job's `timeout-minutes` is **330**, above that
  bound and under the 6 h limit, so a job timeout cannot be caused by row values.
  **Estimates, not measurements:** the absolute bound is arithmetic over the plan's
  row counts and hard limits, and the expected cost of 30 to 60 runner-hours is an
  estimate. The one measured figure is the committed file's solve plus check time,
  58,448 s (16.2 CPU-hours) on `aarch64-apple-darwin`.

### 9.3 BUILD

The BUILD file runs, in release mode (SAT is slow in a debug build): the unit and
integration tests; `run --tier quick`; `check` of the committed results file;
`table --check`; and `gen_evidence --check` of the fixture. **Which part runs in
CI** (the spec was silent, so the choice is stated here): the quick tier and the
`check` re-run of the *same* quick-tier rows, in a few seconds of solver time;
the full tier is a recorded offline run that BUILD never executes. The quick tier
takes the smallest knob of each experiment at every recorded seed (the plan's
`quick` flag), and asserts **verdicts and SAT controls, never timings** (timings
differ per host; asserting them would be a flaky gate). **The quick tier fails
BUILD on any row that is `unknown`, `killed`, `oom`, `crashed`, `sat` (an UNSAT
shape that is not) or `refused`**, with one exception: the E1 `N = 11` row, which
*must* be `refused` with W0706 (a missing refusal fails). A B1 row must be
`collapsed` (an identical sequence that does not collapse is a guard failure),
and every UNSAT, collapsed or enumeration row must carry a passing G3 control. A
guard violation (section 9.4) fails BUILD. The full tier is run by hand or
nightly; its output is committed by a person who reviews it (section 9.6, "what
`check` can and cannot verify"). **Amended by VOS-028c3c-1 (planned, not built):** the
full tier is no longer a local, by-hand run. Its recording and its full
verification run in the workflow below.

**Cadence of full verification (pre-registered by VOS-028c3c-1).** BUILD and the
per-PR `check` (the quick tier, as above) are unchanged. Full verification (`check
--full`, sharded as in section 9.2) runs in `vos-capacity-full.yml` (VOS-028c3c-3):

* **Weekly, and on dispatch (owner decision, 2026-11-24, "Weekly only"):** the verify runs once a week, unconditionally, and on manual dispatch. A change to any input does not by itself make the file invalid; the verify decides (VOS-D141). There is no gate and no gated nightly, so there is no baseline run, no key over the input ids and nothing to skip. Cost was the reason: each verify is 64 shards and about 30 to 60 runner-hours, and the 46 input entries changed on 20 of the 30 days before the decision, so a nightly gated on them would have run most nights. **Paused 2026-11-26 (owner, "Pause until re-record (Recommended)"): the weekly schedule is removed and the workflow runs on manual dispatch only, because a weekly verify of recording 45486250172 cannot go green (VOS-D149); the schedule is restored once a new recording verifies green (VOS-028c3e).**
* **`max-parallel: 8`**, its own concurrency group with `cancel-in-progress: false`
  (a verify that is running is never cancelled by a newer one; but GitHub keeps only the
  **newest pending** run per group, so a weekly tick can cancel a queued record dispatch.
  Operator rule, VOS-028c3c-3a: do not dispatch record while a run is in flight, and log
  a cancelled pending dispatch by hand), and a cron time off
  `ci.yml`'s slots (`0 0 * * *` and `0 6,12,18 * * *`).
* **Not nightly**, gated or not: at `max-parallel: 8` the absolute bound of
  section 9.2 (about 220 runner-hours) is about 27.5 h of wall clock, which is longer
  than a day (an estimate: 220 / 8), and the owner chose a weekly cadence for cost (above).
* **This supersedes the owner's accepted gap of 2026-11-20** (section 9.12, round 3:
  "no scheduled or per-PR `check --full` job"). There is now a scheduled `check
  --full`; it is a weekly one, not a per-PR one, and the release gate of section 9.12
  is satisfied by a green run of `check --full --at-checkout` at the tree being released (VOS-028c3d).

### 9.4 Validity guards, on every row

These are the lessons of the VER-P030 audit and of the calibration; a row that
fails a guard is *invalid*, never a capacity datum.

**G1. Collapse accounting, measured from the AIG.** Record the number of AND
nodes in the miter's **output cone** (`ands`), computed by walking the `Aig` from
the miter output, not taken from the generator, and a `collapsed` flag: the CNF
has at most two clauses, or the solver used zero conflicts. A collapsed row is
reported with `result = "collapsed"` and excluded from every threshold and table
statistic. Experiment B1 (a sequence against itself, divergence 0%) *must* report
collapsed; it measures build time and memory only. `collapsed_reason` is the
four-value closed enum of the section 4.5 table (`identical-structure`,
`constant-fold`, `frame-untouched`, `propagation-only`) and `check` verifies it
structurally, by the allowed-when rules of that table, from the instance.

**G2. Liveness of generated programs, measured semantically.** Generators keep
programs live: inject a fresh symbolic load every `6` instructions, and cap the
share of entropy-killing operations (`sltu`, shifts, `and` with small constants)
at `20%`. The guard is *measured*, and on **behaviour**, not graph structure (a
cone can mention an input bit and still ignore it):

* **Declared bits** come from the generator's *input list* (the symbolic registers
  and loads it created), enumerated by the tool, never a self-reported count.
* **Sensitivity** is defined on **each side's own output cone** (the code side
  and the reference side separately), **not on the miter output**: an UNSAT miter
  is constant 0 and would wrongly mark every row collapsed. It is measured by
  **random simulation of the cone with per-input-bit flips**: for each declared bit, over `256` random assignments flip
  that bit and see whether any output bit changes; a bit is *sensitive* if it ever
  does. (Where an instance is small, a SAT query "two inputs differing only in
  this bit give different outputs" replaces the sampling.) `entropy_bits` is the
  number of sensitive bits. **As built:** the sampling is the first stage and
  the SAT query decides every bit it did not find sensitive, for a cone of at
  most 4,000 ANDs (and `undecided bits x ANDs` at most 400,000); a larger cone is
  measured by sampling alone, and a very large one by a sample of the bits with
  the count scaled, and the row says so in its `note`. (Sampling alone calls a
  64-bit `pick` dead: a bit matters only when no higher bit is set, which random
  assignments reach for about nine bits of any width.) In the cut form (inputs
  `...@c` and `...@r`, one per side) each side is measured against *its own*
  declared inputs. For a **self-composition** (C1, C2) the output is
  secret-independent by design, so the two cones measured are the two sides of
  the equivalence that cancels the secret (`code(s) XOR ref(s)` taken apart), not
  the output. For a **chain** the counts are summed over the segments that have
  any AND at all; a chain with none is `collapsed`.
* A row is `collapsed` if, on **either** side's output cone (the rule must hold for
  both sides, so it fails closed), fewer than **50% of the declared bits are sensitive**, or
  if the AND count of the miter cone is below **25% of the AND count of the same
  generator at the same length with divergence 100%**. These are the defaults; a
  change to any is a reviewed change.
* The G3 control proves sensitivity **at one site only** (the mutated
  instruction); it does not substitute for this per-bit measure.

This is the guard against the n of 200 or more rows of table B2.

**G3. SAT negative control, per experiment and per mode.** Every UNSAT experiment
has a paired SAT control. The rules:

* the control is the same instance with **one mutated instruction** (or, for
  memory experiments D1 to D4, one mutated access: a wrong index, a dropped
  store, a wrong stride; for E1 and E2, one mutated generated claim that the
  harness must report as failing, and a record whose width bound is violated that
  must be refused);
* it must be **SAT**, and the model is validated: the SAT model **satisfies the
  CNF** (re-evaluated by a small CNF evaluator, not trusting the solver's flag);
* its counterexample is **replayed**: the code side on the concrete
  `riscv-simulator`, the reference side on an **independent concrete evaluator**
  (written separately from the stepper; it must not call into the stepper's
  circuit), and the two outputs must differ;
* a **control per mode**: the cut experiments (B3, D4) have a control in the
  incremental mode and another in the certified mode, and the self-composition
  experiments (C1, C2) have a control that is a leaking variant, replayed by
  running two concrete runs that agree on public inputs and differ in trace;
* a control that is UNSAT, or whose model does not satisfy the CNF, or whose
  counterexample does not replay, fails the row. The control instance has its own
  digest (`control_instance_sha256`).

A single counterexample does not show the stepper agrees with the simulator; the
real test is the randomized differential of section 7 (the probe has a quick-tier
differential on 1,000 random states per opcode for the same reason).

**G4. Determinism.** The generator is seeded; the seed is a recorded field. A row
reproduces bit-for-bit (same DIMACS digest) from `(experiment, knob, seed)`.

### 9.5 Measurements per row, limits and the timeout

Per row (see the schema of section 9.6): `ands`, `vars`, `clauses`, `result`
(`unsat`, `sat`, `unknown`, `collapsed`, `refused`, `killed`, `oom`, `crashed`),
`conflicts`, `propagations`, `solve_ms` (wall), `drat_bytes`, `check_ms` (wall)
and `check_work` (the `Accepted.work` of `drat-check`), `pass_default_limits`
(checked under `Limits::default()`; the probe never raises limits, so a row too
big for the defaults records `false` and the reason), `peak_rss_kb`.

**Resource limits** (the probe runs hostile-sized instances by design; these keep
it from taking the machine down, and a limit that trips is a red row, never a
silent drop):

1. **Conflict cap with proof logging.** Whenever proof logging is on, the solve
   stops at **1,000,000 conflicts** (the proof is held in memory, about 350 MB per
   million conflicts); past it the row is `unknown`.
2. **Wall timeout, 120 s per row**, as a loop over `set_conflict_budget(Some(20_000))`
   with a clock check between slices (`cdcl-sat/src/solver.rs:422`; the budget
   counts down per slice, so it is reset each iteration; as built a slice also
   ends at 4 x 10^8 watch-list visits, `Solver::set_work_budget`, so the clock is
   read about every second even on a million-clause formula where 20,000
   conflicts would take minutes). A timeout is `result = "unknown"`, cap `wall`,
   with the conflicts used so far. A chain also stops between two obligations.
   **Chain budget (as built, VOS-028c2; VOS-D095).** A split or chain (B3, D3, D4,
   later B5 and D5) is one row, so the 120 s wall bounds the *sum* of its
   obligations, although 8.3 allows a feasible entry about ten minutes in total.
   Such a row gets a **600 s row budget** while each obligation keeps its 120 s; the
   hard kill moves with it (600 s plus 30 s for the control stage plus 150 s of
   slack). A chain already stops at its first obligation that is not UNSAT, which
   bounds the larger budget. The row records the budget in a field `wall_s`, omitted
   when it is 120, so rows that do not change stay byte-identical. As built, an
   obligation's deadline is the earlier of the row's deadline and 120 s after it
   starts (`exp::obligation_deadline`); the parent's kill is `hard_limit_for(wall_s)`;
   `validate` accepts `wall_s` only as the row budget of a chain (`row_wall_s`: 600 on
   B3, D3 and D4, and later D5) and a chain row
   recorded before the budget existed (no field) stays valid; `compare_rerun` does not
   compare it (it is the budget, not a measurement).
   **B5's walls (VOS-028c3b, owner direction 2026-11-23).** B5 is the one exception to
   the 600 s: its row wall is **1,800 s**, each lemma solve and the final obligation
   **600 s**, and its G3 control has its own **600 s and 1,000,000-conflict cap**; the
   hard kill is 1,800 s plus 600 s plus 150 s of slack (2,550 s). They are measurement
   walls, not gates: they decide only whether a run answers or ends `unknown`, and
   change no gate number of 8.3 or 8.6 (the 600 s total of B5 is the sweep's elapsed
   time, the `sweep_ms` metric: instance build through verdict, G3 control excluded, a
   definition fixed before any recorded run, enforced fail-closed by `route_green`).
3. **Whole-process-group kill.** The row runs as a child in its **own process
   group**; the parent kills the *group* on the wall timeout, covering **every
   stage**: instance generation and CNF writing, the solve, and `drat-check`, not
   only the solver loop. A stage that never returns (a pathological generator, a
   checker running to its work limit) is thereby bounded too. Result `killed`.
   As built the kill is at **300 s**: 120 s of solving, 30 s for the control
   stage and 150 s of slack, because the checker's own work limit (2^32 visits)
   bounds one check to about two minutes; a row that merely needs its check is
   red for the right reason, not `killed`.
4. **Enforced memory cap per child**: the parent polls the child's resident set
   **every 50 ms** (`/usr/bin/time` output is only a final figure) and kills the
   group above **2 GB** (result `oom`). The row records **which cap tripped**
   (`cap`: `conflicts`, `wall`, `rss` or `none`), so a proof-logging run that hits
   the 1M-conflict cap near 1.8 GB resident (`unknown`) is not confused with the
   2 GB RSS cap (`oom`). `RLIMIT_AS` is *not* relied on: it is unreliable on
   macOS (it does not bound resident memory the way it does on Linux).
5. **A crash** (a panic, a signal) is `crashed`, with the exit status in `note`.
6. **Peak RSS must be parsed.** If the `/usr/bin/time` figure cannot be parsed the
   row **fails**; it is never defaulted to 0.

`unknown`, `killed`, `oom` and `crashed` are all **red** and are never dropped
from the table: they are the data that says "does not fit".

**Two modes for cut experiments.** Where a cut-point experiment is measured,
both are run and recorded:

* `mode = "incremental"`: one solver, assumptions per segment, no proof logging
  (fast; the form of the advisor's table B3); informational;
* `mode = "certified"`: one fresh CNF per segment with the hypothesis as clauses,
  DRAT logged and checked under default limits (the form that would ship;
  section 6.1 route A). **The decision uses the certified numbers.**

### 9.6 The result file

`code/specs/data/vos30-capacity-results.json`, one object:

```
{
  "schema": 2,
  "tool": { "name": "vos-capacity-probe", "version": "<semver>",
            "binary_sha256": "<sha256 of the running executable>",
            "dirty_tree": <true|false> },
  "host": { "triple": "<target triple>", "rustc": "<rustc --version>", "os": "..." },
  "revisions": { "bit-blaster": "<git rev>", "cdcl-sat": "<git rev>",
                 "drat-check": "<git rev>", "riscv-simulator": "<git rev>",
                 "repo": "<git rev>" },
  "limits": { "timeout_s": 120, "conflict_slice": 20000, "proof_conflict_cap": 1000000,
              "rss_cap_kb": 2097152, "seeds": 5,
              "drat_check": { "max_vars": 1048576, "max_work": 4294967296 } },
  "regeneration": null | { "reviewer": "<name>", "host": "<host>", "run_id": "<digits>",
                           "run_attempt": "<digits>", "head_sha": "<40 hex>",
                           "unsigned_sha256": "<64 hex>",
                           "verified": { "run_id": "<digits>", "run_attempt": "<digits>",
                                         "head_sha": "<40 hex>", "verify_sha256": "<64 hex>",
                                         "trees": { <as tool.trees, where the verify ran> } } },
                                                               (written only by `sign`, section 9.2)
  "merges": [ { "rev", "binary_sha256", "dirty_tree", "experiment", "where", "rows", "at" } ],
                                              (optional; one entry per `merge`, section 9.2)
  "rows": [ Row, ... ]
}
```

`Row`:

| Field | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `experiment`, `knob` | string, object | e.g. `"B3"`, `{ "length": 100, "divergence": 0.25, "cut": "every8" }` |
| `mode` | string | `monolithic`, `incremental`, `certified`, `split`, `enum` |
| `seed`, `seeds` | integer | the seed of this row, and the seeds run for the knob (at least 5); the table uses the maximum (section 8.3) |
| `instance_sha256` | string | SHA-256 of the DIMACS text |
| `drat_sha256` | string or null | SHA-256 of the DRAT text |
| `control_instance_sha256` | string | SHA-256 of the control's DIMACS |
| `ands`, `vars`, `clauses` | integer | size of the miter cone (measured from the AIG) and CNF |
| `entropy_bits`, `declared_bits` | integer | symbolic input bits the output cone depends on, and the declared inputs (G2) |
| `result` | string | `unsat`, `sat`, `unknown`, `collapsed`, `refused`, `killed`, `oom`, `crashed`; and `pass`, `fail` for the enumeration rows E1 and E2 |
| `collapsed_reason` | string or null | the enum of section 4.5 |
| `control` | object | `{ "result": "sat", "model_checks_cnf": true, "replayed": true }` (G3) |
| `conflicts`, `propagations` | integer | solver counters (summed over steps for chains) |
| `worst_step_conflicts` | integer or null | for chains and splits |
| `solve_ms` | number | wall time of the solve |
| `drat_bytes` | integer or null | DRAT size |
| `check_ms`, `check_work` | number, integer or null | `drat-check` wall and work |
| `pass_default_limits` | boolean or null | accepted under `Limits::default()` |
| `peak_rss_kb` | integer | child process peak RSS; a row with an unparsed value is invalid |
| `green` | boolean | computed by the tool from section 8.3, never typed |
| `cap` | string | which limit ended the row: `none`, `conflicts`, `wall` or `rss` |
| `note` | string | a refusal reason or an anomaly |
| `wall_s` | integer, optional | the row budget in seconds when it is not 120 (as built, VOS-028c2; section 9.5) |

**Additions of VOS-028c2 (as built) and VOS-028c3 (planned), all optional so recorded rows keep their bytes:**
`wall_s` on a row (above); in the `tool` object and in each `merges` entry a `trees`
object of content-addressed **input ids** (`git rev-parse HEAD:<path>`: a tree id for a
directory, a blob id for a file), one per **build input** rather than per crate (VOS-D139,
VOS-D140; section 9.12), keyed `<crate>/<relative path>`: `vos-capacity-probe/src`,
`vos-capacity-probe/Cargo.toml`, `vos-capacity-probe/Cargo.lock`, `bit-blaster/src`,
`bit-blaster/Cargo.toml`, `cdcl-sat/src`, `cdcl-sat/Cargo.toml`, `drat-check/src`,
`drat-check/Cargo.toml`, `riscv-simulator/src`, `riscv-simulator/Cargo.toml`, the two
generated harnesses the probe `include!`s
(`vos-sched-fp-bitmap/tests/generated/warrant_evidence.rs`,
`vos-probe-ready/tests/generated/warrant_evidence.rs`) and the two documents it
`include_bytes!`s (`specs/vos-sched-fp-bitmap.warrant`, `specs/vos-probe-ready.warrant`);
`src` and `Cargo.toml` of **every other path package in the probe's dependency closure**
(read from the probe's `Cargo.lock`: `arithmetic`, `bounded-json`, `sha256`, `test-bootstrap`,
`warrant-grammar`, `zeroize`, `cpu-simulator`, `grammar-tools`, `logic-gates`, `riscv-encoder`,
`transistors`, `vos-probe-ready`, `vos-sched-contract`, `vos-sched-fp-bitmap`, the E1 path
among them), because `Cargo.lock` does not pin a path dependency's content; the two
`#[path]` fixtures of `test-bootstrap`; and the repository-root `.cargo/config.toml`
(`repo/.cargo/config.toml`, the only `.cargo/config*` on an ancestor path of the probe). A
probe test recomputes the path-package set from `Cargo.lock` and fails when one lacks its
entries. What stays uncovered: registry crates (pinned by `Cargo.lock`) and the toolchain
(pinned by `mise.toml` and the workflow). The list is `TREE_ENTRIES` in the probe's `src/meta.rs`, and the CI guard's map is tested
equal to it. **Left out, with the reason:** `tests/`, `benches/` (not linked into the
measuring binary), `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `BUILD`, `BUILD_windows`,
`required_capabilities.json`, `vos.toml` (documentation and build-tool metadata): an edit to
any of them cannot change a recorded value, so it must not stale the recording. None of the
five crates has a `build.rs`; one that gains it joins the list. `validate` requires exactly these names in the
`tool` object, and hex object ids; a `merges` entry may instead keep the five whole-crate
names the 0.1.0 and early 0.2.0 files recorded (`vos-capacity-probe`, `bit-blaster`,
`cdcl-sat`, `drat-check`, `riscv-simulator`), as history: they are never compared with a
working tree (`check` prints one advisory `stale:` line for such an entry and `check --full`
refuses it, as it did a stale id); in a merges entry `"kind": "add"`
(section 9.2; `validate` accepts no other kind); and, planned for VOS-028c3, in a B5
row's `metrics` the counts `lemmas`, `candidates`, `refuted` and `escalations`,
compared by the re-run like every other metric, and the timing `sweep_ms` (the B5 total of
8.6.3; nondeterministic, so present on both sides but not compared by value). The `limits` object is unchanged (the
chain budget is recorded per row), so a merge rewrites no byte of it.

`dirty_tree`, `binary_sha256` and the regeneration banner are **self-reported by
the tool**: nothing stops an edited tool or file from writing the values it
likes. Reviewer regeneration is therefore a **process control**, not a technical
one; the results file is evidence only after a named person has regenerated it.
The banner `sign` writes is just as self-reported: the tool binds it to the rows and to
the run (section 9.2), not to the person who typed `--reviewer`.

**What `check` verifies, and what it cannot.**

* Schema and guards, always, on every row: every UNSAT row has a replayed control
  whose model satisfies the CNF; no `collapsed`, `unknown`, `killed`, `oom` or
  `crashed` row is `green`; `green` equals the section 8.3 predicate recomputed
  from the other fields; peak RSS present; seeds at least 5 per knob;
  `dirty_tree` is `false` for a committed file (a dirty-tree run may not be
  committed).
* **Re-runs**: `check` **re-runs every quick-tier row** (the smallest knob of each
  experiment, deterministic from the recorded seed) and compares the instance
  digest, the verdict, the control result and the **conflict count** (equal, since
  the solver is deterministic); any difference fails. It also re-checks every
  recorded DRAT of those rows with `drat-check`.
* **Full-tier rows are not verifiable by `check`** (they take hours and their
  seconds are host-dependent). It validates their schema and guards only. A
  reviewer **regenerates the full tier** on a stated host before the numbers are
  accepted, and the PR says so. A result file that a reviewer has not regenerated
  carries the banner "full tier not independently regenerated" in the table.

### 9.7 The experiments

Each experiment lists its shape, sweep and what a verdict is. "Equal knobs" mean
the quick tier uses the smallest value only.

| Id | Shape | Sweep |
|---|---|---|
| **A1** | *Pick miter*: the RV32 `clzN` binary-search sequence against the "highest set member" priority chain | N = 8, 16, 32, 64 |
| **A2** | *Insert/remove miter*: `mask | (1 << p)` against a per-`Bool` update with the `p >= N` guard | N = 8 up to 64 |
| **B1** | A straight-line sequence against itself (divergence 0%) | lengths 200, 500, 1000; **must report `collapsed`**; measures build time and memory only |
| **B2** | *Monolithic*, a share of instructions encoded differently in the reference | length 25, 50, 100, 200, 500, 1000, times divergence 5%, 25%, 100% |
| **B3** | B2 with cut points: per instruction, then per block every 8 and every 32. **Total and worst-step conflicts, certified mode: the number that funds VOS-029.** | same grid; both modes of section 9.5, at least 5 seeds |
| **B4** | *Arithmetic with no cut point*: n-term re-association; `x*5` against `(x<<2)+x`; `base + i*12` against shift-add | n = 3 to 7 at W = 32 (n = 2 is one adder against itself); W = 8, 16, 32 |
| **C1** | *Self-composition shape* of an A1 or B body, **as built**: two copies, public inputs shared, secrets fresh; the observable is the public output `pub_chain XOR (f_code(s) XOR f_ref(s))`, secret-independent only because `f_code` and `f_ref` are *equivalent* (section 9.10). So **C1 on a B base contains two B2 equivalences by construction and can never be greener than B2**; it is not code-against-code two-safety and builds no pc, address or count trace (the original description, after `prove_with_custom_inputs` and VER20 section 2.3, is not what was measured; code-against-code trace shapes are backlog VOS-D096). C2 is the only experiment whose observable is an address trace | the same sweeps as A and B |
| **C2** | A secret-dependent load index (**must be SAT**) and a padded constant-time variant (UNSAT) | T = 8, 16, 64 |
| **D1** | *Memory mux*: T words, k chained symbolic load-modify-store at offsets `i + j` of one symbolic base index, the `sym.rs` eq/ite chain against a mux tree | T = 4, 8, 16, 64, 256, k = 1, 2, 4, 8, 16 |
| **D2** | D1 with concrete offsets | same grid |
| **D3** | D1 case-split on the index: T obligations, reported as **sum and max** | same grid |
| **D4** | Both sides share the mux structure, with cut points after each access | same grid |
| **B3 additions** (registered, VOS-028c3c commit A) | B3 with `cut` = `every2` and `every4`, both modes | the same 6 x 3 grid as B3; 360 rows, none quick; fixes the cut density K of 8.6.2 |
| **B5** (registered, VOS-028c3c commit A) | *SAT sweeping with certified lemmas* on B2 with the `fuse` knob (8.6.3); a research experiment, judged by route-green | length 25 (quick), 200, 1,000, times divergence 25%, 100%, times `fuse` 1, 2, 4, 8; 24 knobs, 120 rows |
| **D5** (registered, VOS-028c3c commit A) | *Memory as index split plus a cut per access plus touched-word comparison*, the frame a separate structural obligation (8.6.3) | T = 4, 16, 64, 256, k = 1, 4, 16, split = `index`, `none`; 24 knobs, 120 rows, quick T = 4, k = 1 |
| **E1** | *Enumeration throughput* of the generated by-value harness: the real `vos-sched-fp-bitmap` at N = 8 (binder products 256 and 65,536) and the fixture `vos-probe-ready` (65,536 in the 8-`Bool` form and in the `Impl(mask: U8)` form, 262,144 for insert at N = 10, 393,216); **N = 11 must be refused (W0706)**. Run-only cases per second (the harness is compiled into the probe: compile time is the probe's build). G3 control: the same claim against a broken adapter, which the harness must report failing; the refusal row's control is the same document at N = 10, which must be accepted | as stated |
| **E2** | *Enumeration memory*: `k` binders of `Bytes<W>` (W = 1, 8, 64, 256, 4096; k = 1 to 4): the generator's own accounting of cases and domain bytes (`domain::resolve_binders`), and for an accepted claim the materialised domains and the counted product; where the 64 MiB domain bound (VER04m E10) bites before 400,000 cases. G3 control: a binder wider than the 4096 cap must be refused (W0707) | as stated |
| **F** | DRAT size and check time on every UNSAT row: pass under default limits, bytes per conflict, check/solve ratio | derived constants printed under the generated table (section 9.8), not a row of its own |

Notes on a few of them, because the shape explains the choice:

* **A1** is where "CLZ is not a cost driver" is confirmed (table A); it is the
  entry-level `pick` shape.
* **B1** is a *negative* result by design: it demonstrates guard G1, that an
  identical sequence collapses, and measures only what building a large
  sequence costs.
* **B2 against B3** is the pair that decides the route. B2 is the cliff; B3 is
  what a cut does about it, at three cut granularities, with certification.
* **B4** isolates arithmetic structure (re-association, strength reduction)
  from instruction count, which is the cost driver identified in section 2.2.
* **D1, D2, D3, D4** triangulate the memory story: chained symbolic accesses
  (red), concrete offsets (the target), case-split (the substitute for arrays)
  and cut points between accesses.
* **E1** sits beside the solver rows because the X1 bar is enumeration, and
  its throughput and its refusal at N = 11 are the X1 reduced-instance
  evidence (VOS01 section 5.6). The fixture is committed so the numbers come from
  the real generated harness.

### 9.8 The generated table and its check mode

`vos-capacity-probe table` reads the committed results file and emits, for each
experiment-knob group, one line that is the **worst seed's own row** (section 8.3;
the tie-break is the lowest seed), with two group-level cells: the seeds (counted
of total, and the distinct instances among the counted) and the maximum solve time
and resident memory over the seeds:

```
| Experiment | Knob | Mode | ANDs | Vars | Clauses | Entropy bits | Seeds | Conflicts (total / worst) | Solve | DRAT | Check work | Default limits | RSS | Control | Max over seeds (solve / RSS) | Verdict |
```

`Control` is `replayed` or the failure; `Verdict` is `GREEN`, `red (reason)`,
`collapsed`, `unknown (timeout)`, `killed`, `oom` or `crashed`, computed from
section 8.3. The generated block **begins with a fixed banner and the validity
limits** (section 8.4, all of them), so a table can never be read without them:

```
PROXY: synthetic hand-written code with synthetic divergence, not compiler
output. These numbers bound solver capacity per obligation shape; they do not
prove any entry verifiable. Seeds >= 5; each line is its worst seed's own row. <full-tier regeneration
banner if applicable>
```

The table is placed in this document between a pair of HTML-comment markers
named `vos30-table:begin` and `vos30-table:end` (they are written out only once,
around the block itself, because the checker finds the first pair; the markers
were not present in the spec until a generated table existed, because an empty
block would look like a result). `table --check`
regenerates the block and compares it byte for byte with the spec, failing on
**drift**. It **also fails if section 8.5 states a decision** (one of the three
rows of section 8.3, after the words `Decision (VOS-028b)` in bold) **while the
generated block is absent or empty**, if the stated row is not the row the results
give, if the results file is absent while the block is present, and if the results
file exists but the block does not. **No number in the table is hand-typed.**
VOS-028b keeps the tables of section 8.2 as history under a *superseded* banner
(so that the comparison with the advisor stays readable) and states the decision of
section 8.5 from the generated table.

**The generated table** (written by `vos-capacity-probe table --write`; never edit
between the markers by hand, `table --check` fails on any difference):

<!-- vos30-table:begin -->
PROXY: synthetic hand-written code with synthetic divergence, not compiler
output. These numbers bound solver capacity per obligation shape; they do not
prove any entry verifiable. Seeds >= 5; each line is its worst seed's own row (ties: lowest seed), with the maximum solve time and resident memory over the seeds in the last column but one. Full tier not independently regenerated.

Validity limits (VOS30 section 8.4), printed with every number:

1. Hand-written code is not compiler output: no register allocation, spills, fusion or inlining; real code may be easier or harder.
2. The divergence is synthetic: the reference differs from the code in controlled proportions (5%, 25%, 100% of the live instructions that have an alternative encoding), not as a real compiler's would.
3. Trap entry, CSRs and `mret` are not modelled: only the ALU, `lw`/`lbu`/`sw`/`sb` and forward branches with a concrete PC.
4. The probe bounds solver capacity per obligation shape; it does not prove any entry verifiable.
5. One machine, one solver build: seconds are host-specific, conflicts and work are the portable part.
6. The probe never raises the `drat-check` limits: a proof the default limits reject is a red row (the advisor's numbers raised them, so every proof there was checkable).
7. The D instances share one symbolic base index; `k` independent symbolic indices are not measured, and the index has `log2 T` bits so no out-of-range case arises.
8. Liveness is decided exactly only for cones of at most 4,000 ANDs; a larger cone's entropy rests on 256 random assignments (a row says so in its note).
9. Both sides run on one stepper: a stepper bug that affects both alike is invisible to the miter (the checks are the differential against the simulator, the independent evaluator and the replay of every control).
10. "Five seeds" means five distinct instances only where the Seeds column says so: a deterministic shape (A, C on an A1 base, D with a concrete index) may build the same circuit for several seeds. A group's line is its worst seed's own row, chosen by non-green, then most conflicts, then longest solve, then lowest seed, with every cell from that one row except the two group-level cells (the distinct-instance count and the maximum solve and resident memory over the seeds); a proof or check that was never produced shows `-`, unmeasured.

Run: `vos-capacity-probe` 0.2.0, `rustc 1.100.0 (0ef1bc386 2026-11-09)`, Linux 7.0.0-1017-azure; the `check` re-run reproduces the quick-tier rows.

| Experiment | Knob | Mode | ANDs | Vars | Clauses | Entropy bits | Seeds (distinct instances) | Conflicts (total / worst) | Solve | DRAT | Check work | Default limits | RSS | Control | Max over seeds (solve / RSS) | Verdict |

Derived constants (experiment F), from the counted monolithic UNSAT rows of at least 1,000 conflicts:

* Solver throughput: 6,888 to 164,020 conflicts per second.
* DRAT size: 29 to 329 bytes per conflict.
* `drat-check` time: 0.3x to 5.7x the solve time.
* Checker work: 851 to 37,362 literal visits per conflict.
* Default `drat-check` work limit (2^32): the largest proof accepted has 457,844 conflicts, the smallest rejected for work has 82,835.
* Resident memory: 0.6 to 1.0 GB per million conflicts (rows of at least 100,000 conflicts).

Section 8.3 evaluated on these rows: **row 1, Infeasible: re-plan X3**.

* D3 at T = 8, k = 16: NOT green; worst conflicts 519397, worst solve 22010 ms
* C1 at 200 instructions, 25% divergence: NOT green; worst conflicts 1000046, worst solve 98542 ms
<!-- vos30-table:end -->

### 9.9 Timeline

The probe was cheap to write (the stepper, the shapes and the experiment harness
are a few thousand lines with their tests) and the full tier takes hours; BUILD
runs only the quick tier. Interpretation is part of the PR: it ends with the
decision sentence of section 8.5, filled in. **Status: done in the VOS-028b PR**
(stacked on #19571); the full tier was run by the implementer and still needs
regeneration by a named reviewer on a stated host before the numbers count as
evidence (section 9.6).

### 9.10 Implementation decisions of VOS-028b

Everything below is what the implementation fixed where this section left a choice
open. Each item is binding on `check` and on a later re-run.

**Instances.** One seed makes one program; `(experiment, knob, seed)` fixes the
DIMACS text (G4). For the deterministic shapes (A, C, D) the seed shuffles the
**order in which the AIG inputs are created**, i.e. the variable numbering, which
is the one honest source of solver variance they have; for the generated programs
(B) it also selects the program, the divergence sites and the control mutation.

| Id | Code side | Reference side |
|---|---|---|
| A1 | the branch-free RV32I `clz32` binary search (five `srli/sltiu/slli/sll/add` rounds and a zero fix-up), then `r = 32 - clz` (N = 64: two words and a mask select) | a priority chain `ite(bit N-1, N, ite(bit N-2, N-1, ... ite(bit 0, 1, 0)))`, the position of the highest set member plus one, 0 when empty |
| A2 | branch-free insert and remove on an N-bit mask (`sltiu` guard, `sll` of 1, mask select; N = 64: two words), both outputs compared | per-`Bool` update `ite(p == i, b, m_i)`; `p >= N` changes nothing |
| B | a seeded straight-line RV32I sequence over `x1..x8` with a fresh symbolic `lw` every 6 instructions (G2), at most 20% entropy-killing operations | the same sequence as an `Op` list built through the same `bv` operations, except that a seeded share of the instructions that have an alternative is encoded differently (adder identities, reversed barrel stages, borrow-bit comparisons, commuted multiplier) |
| B3 | segments of 1, 8 or 32 instructions, each in its own AIG; every live-in register and memory word is **two fresh inputs, one per side, joined by an equality premise** (the hypothesis as clauses); all registers and words the segment writes are compared | as B |
| B4 | n-term re-association `((a+b)+c)+d` against `a+(b+(c+d))`, `x*5` against `(x<<2)+x` (the multiplier by a symbolic register premised equal to 5), and `base + i*12` against shift-add; width W by comparing the low W bits of zero-extended W-bit inputs | as stated |
| C1 | two copies of an A1 or B body with public inputs shared and secrets fresh; the public output is `pub_chain XOR (f_code(s) XOR f_ref(s))`, secret-independent only *semantically*, so the proof is a real cancellation, not a structural one | the same with `f_code(s)` alone (a leak), the SAT control |
| C2 | the padded variant loads all T words at constant addresses after a semantically cancelled secret perturbation of the address (UNSAT); the leaking variant loads `table[s & (T-1)]` (SAT) | observable = the address trace |
| D | `k` load-add-store accesses at word offsets `i + j` (mod T) of one symbolic base index `i` over a T-word table: the code side is the `sym.rs`-style eq/ite chain over a store log, the reference a per-word array with a binary mux tree; the data operation is the code's `add` against the reference's alternative encoding of it (so that the concrete-offset rows are not collapsed) | as stated |
| D2, D3, D4 | D2: `i` a constant (concrete offsets). D3: D2 for every `i = 0 .. T-1` as T obligations, `conflicts` the sum and `worst_step_conflicts` the max. D4: per access, fresh code and reference tables joined by equality premises, both sides built with the same mux structure; both modes | |

**Limits of the D shape** (item 7 of section 8.4): the k accesses share one
symbolic base index, as the fields of one thread-table entry do; k *independent*
symbolic indices (a product of T^k cases when split) are not measured, and `i` has
log2 T bits, so the out-of-range case of section 4.3 does not arise.

**Which part runs in CI.** BUILD runs the unit tests, `run --tier quick` and
`check` of the committed results file. The quick tier is the smallest knob of
every experiment at all recorded seeds (at least 5); `check` re-runs exactly those
rows and compares the digest, verdict, control and conflict count (section 9.6).
The full tier is a recorded offline run, committed by a person who regenerated it
(section 9.6); BUILD never runs it.

**Fields added to the row** (section 9.6): `tier` (`quick` or `full`), `steps`
(the number of obligations of a chain or split), `worst_step_solve_ms`, and
`metrics` (an object of experiment-specific numbers: E1 and E2 counts and rates, the
divergence actually applied). For a chain or split row `solve_ms` and `check_ms`
are totals, `drat_bytes`, `check_work` and `pass_default_limits` describe the
**worst obligation** (the green predicate of section 8.3 is per obligation), and the
solve-time clause of green uses `worst_step_solve_ms`.

**Timeouts.** The solver loop stops itself at 120 s (cap `wall`, result `unknown`);
the control stage has its own 30 s; the parent kills the process group at 300 s
if the child is still running in any stage (result `killed`, cap `wall`).

**Deviations from the text above, with the reason.**

* E1 and E2 rows have no solver fields (`null`); their "seeds" are repetitions
  (the counts are deterministic, the rates are samples, and the table shows the
  slowest). Their `result` is `pass`, `fail` or `refused` (the closed set of
  section 9.6 gains `pass` and `fail` for them) and their control is `caught`:
  a broken adapter that the harness reports failing, or a binder width over the
  cap that the generator refuses. A refused E1 row carries `result = "refused"`
  and `green = false`; BUILD expects exactly that row (section 9.3).
* **B4 starts at three terms.** `n = 2` is `a + b` against `a + b`: one adder,
  hash-consed into itself, a collapse by construction and not a re-association.
* **D sweep.** `T` in 4, 8, 16, 64, 256 and `k` in 1, 2, 4, 8, 16 (a grid of 25
  knobs per experiment); D4 and B3 run both modes.
* **The incremental mode is informational** and logs no proof: its `green` is the
  same predicate without the DRAT and default-limit clauses, and the decision
  never reads it.
* **A chain's `green`** is the section 8.3 predicate per obligation: the worst
  obligation's conflicts and solve time, the largest DRAT, the checker accepting
  every obligation. The total is in `conflicts` and `solve_ms`.
* **E1 digests the document.** An E1 row's `instance_sha256` is the SHA-256 of the
  Warrant document the harness was generated from, not of the generated text: a
  regeneration of a harness by a newer generator must not break a probe whose
  claim set is unchanged, while a changed claim changes the document and the
  case counts.
* **The real scheduler's harness installs its allocation observer as the
  probe's global allocator** (a generated `unsafe impl GlobalAlloc`, inert
  outside an observed call: one thread-local check per allocation). It changes no
  conflict or work count; the probe cannot avoid it without editing a generated
  file (backlog VOS-D091).

### 9.11 The decision procedure, as built

`decide` (and the line printed under the generated table) applies section 8.3 to
the rows. The definitions it fixes, where 8.3 left them to the implementation:

* A **group** is every seed of one `(experiment, mode, knob)`. A **counted** row
  is one that is not `collapsed`. A group is **green** iff at least one row is
  counted and every counted row is green. A group that is missing from the file,
  or whose every seed is collapsed, is **not green** (unclassified is red).
* **Row 1** (infeasible) holds iff any of these required shapes is not green, at
  the finest decomposition tested: A1 at N = 32; D3 at T = 8 with k = 16 (one
  obligation per index); B3 in certified mode with a cut after every instruction
  at 200 instructions and 25% divergence; C1 on a B body of 200 instructions at
  25% divergence.
* **Row 2** (feasible, reduced and mitigated) holds, if row 1 does not, iff any
  of: B3 (certified, 25% divergence) is green at 1,000 instructions with a cut
  after every instruction but not with a cut every 32 (green only with cuts at 8
  instructions or fewer), or is green at 200 instructions but not at 1,000 (only
  up to about 200); D3 at k = 16 is not green at T = 64 or at T = 256 (green only
  for T at most 16); B4 re-association is not green at some n of 5, 6, 7 (only n at
  most 4).
* **Row 3** (feasible as planned) holds, if neither row 1 nor row 2 does, iff all
  of: A1 and A2 are green at N = 32 and 64; B3 (certified, 25% divergence) is
  green at 1,000 instructions for some cut granularity, with a worst step of at
  most 10,000 conflicts and a total (solve plus check) of at most 60 s; D2 and D3
  are green at T = 64, k = 16; C1 on a 1,000-instruction body at 25% divergence is
  green.
* If none of the three holds, the outcome is row 1 and the printed reasons say
  which row-3 condition failed.

The unit tests of `results.rs` carry a negative control for every clause.

### 9.12 The re-plan's implementation and decision procedure (VOS-028c2 as built; VOS-028c3 planned)

The VOS-028c3 part of this section is not built; it is the plan that 8.6 is judged by.
As each item lands, the PR that builds it replaces "planned" by the as-built decision,
as 9.10 and 9.11 did for VOS-028b.

**VOS-028c2: the probe, no new experiment (as built).** Version 0.2.0. The code is
in `src/runner.rs` (items 2, 3, 5), `src/spot.rs` and `src/cost.rs` (item 1), `src/results.rs` (the
knob set, `wall_s`, `trees`, tier and coverage rules, timing comparison), `src/cli.rs` (`merge --add`,
`check`, `--jobs`, the capped reader and atomic writer), and the negative controls are in
`tests/hardening.rs` and, for the findings of the security review below, `tests/review_fixes.rs`. The re-recording of B3, D3 and D4
below is a separate step, run on the committed tree and merged in a second commit.

* The 600 s chain budget and `wall_s` of 9.5 (VOS-D095); the knobs of B3, D3 and D4
  that ended `unknown (timeout)` are re-recorded with it (`run --tier full --only
  B3,D3,D4 --jobs 8`, then `merge --experiment B3 --ignore-outside`, repeated for D3
  and D4, then `table --write`; the results file in a second commit, so the tree that
  ran was clean). `decide` must still print row 1; if D3 at T = 8, k = 16 changes,
  that is reported and the outcome of 8.5 is not edited.
  **Recorded (done; binary built from commit 0f69ee86, 915 rows: 844 unsat, 71 unknown).**
  Of the 129 rows that had ended `unknown (timeout)` under the 120 s wall, **61 now
  finish** (B3 32, D3 14, D4 15, all `unsat`); 4 more reach the conflict cap (B3) and
  **64 still end on the wall**, now the 600 s one (B3 7, D3 2, D4 55: every D4 row at
  t = 256, and D4 incremental at t = 64, k = 16). So the 600 s budget does not rescue
  the t = 256 chains; those stay `unknown` and are not evidence either way. `decide`
  still prints row 1 (Infeasible: re-plan X3). D3 at T = 8, k = 16 is unchanged in
  outcome (NOT green): worst conflicts 503,554 to 519,397 and worst solve 14,278 ms to
  15,046 ms (its two earlier `unknown` seeds now finish `unsat`, at 3.5 million
  conflicts, 99 s). The generated table of 8.5 changed in 182 rows (timings and
  memory of every re-recorded group); 20 verdict cells changed: 15 were `unknown (timeout)` (4 now GREEN: D3 t = 64, k = 16;
  t = 256, k = 4 and k = 8; B3 length 1000, div 100%, every1 incremental; 10 now red; 1
  now `unknown (conflict cap)`), and 5 were already measured (D4 t = 16, k = 16
  incremental turns GREEN, the others stay red with a changed reason list). None
  changes the decision of 8.5.
* `merge --add` of 9.2.
* Hardening (VOS-D097). **(1) Provenance by tree id, not by ancestry; graded MEDIUM.**
  The repository squash-merges, so a recorded `repo` revision is generally not an
  ancestor of HEAD (the results file's own `repo` revision `be35c919f51f` and its
  existing merge's `567282add006` are not ancestors of this section's HEAD, although
  the four per-crate revisions are). A rule "recorded revisions must be ancestors of
  HEAD" would make `check` fail today. New runs and merges instead record `trees`
  (content-addressed ids of the probe's and its four solver crates' build inputs; whole
  crate directories at first, narrowed to inputs by VOS-D139 and VOS-D140, section 9.2).
  * **CI `check`: a non-fatal `stale:` line, plan coverage, and a seeded spot check.**
    `check` prints a `stale:` line when a recorded tree differs from the working tree. It
    stays **non-fatal** on purpose: a fatal rule would couple every edit to a solver crate
    to a regeneration of the results file, which is the VOS-D103 failure mode. Before it
    re-runs anything, `check` applies every rule that needs no instance, and a file that
    fails one is refused at once (finding M2; nothing is built from an unvalidated knob):
    `validate`, `quick_plan_covered` and `full_plan_covered`.
    * **Tier (finding H1).** `validate` requires `tier` to be `quick` or `full` and equal to
      the tier the plan gives the row's `(experiment, mode, knob)`, so a relabelled row can
      neither reach the in-process quick re-run nor leave the sample population.
      `quick_specs` also admits only rows the plan puts in the quick tier.
    * **Coverage (finding H2).** `full_plan_covered` requires the file to hold exactly the
      rows of the full plan (`plan(Tier::Full)`, which includes the quick knobs): none
      missing, none extra, no identity twice, and every group's recorded `seeds` equal to the
      seeds present; `quick_plan_covered` applies the same rule to the quick tier. The
      reviewed exemption list `results::FULL_PLAN_EXEMPT` is the full plan's rows the
      committed file is allowed to lack, each with a reason, and a test pins it to the exact
      set of missing rows. **`FULL_PLAN_EXEMPT` is empty**: the re-recorded file holds all
      of the plan's rows (1,625).
    * **The spot check.** Its sample is **seeded from the SHA-256 of the results file's own
      bytes** (its canonical serialisation) and is **deterministic**: CI has to be
      reproducible. Rows are drawn from the full-tier non-E rows that the **plan** classes
      cheap (`cost::CHEAP`, keyed by `(experiment, mode, knob)`, derived from the committed
      CI recording (run 45486250172) with `CHEAP_MAX_MS` = 3000, the slowest seed's solve plus check; finding M4:
      nothing the file records about a row's time or result decides this, so editing those
      fields cannot get a row skipped), taken in seeded order and re-run, each in a child
      under the limits of item (3), until the total budget `SPOT_BUDGET` = 60 s is spent.
      An expensive row is skipped, counted and printed (`skipped (plan cost class
      expensive): n`). Each child's hard limit is the smaller of its row limit and the
      remaining budget plus `SPOT_SLACK` = 15 s (finding M5); a child that does not finish
      in that time is **not verified**, which is a `check` failure for a cheap-class row,
      never a pass. `check` also fails if fewer than `SPOT_FLOOR` = 16 rows were verified,
      and if fewer than 16 rows are eligible at all (round 2, finding 6: the floor is a plain
      constant, no longer `min(16, eligible)`). Each re-run is compared by `compare_rerun`
      (every deterministic field, see round 2 below); **the CI spot check does not compare
      times or memory**.
    * **A plain `check` re-solves only cheap-class rows (round 2, finding 1).** The plan's
      expensive rows, 1,390 of the 2,000 full-tier non-E rows, **including groups that `decide`
      reads** (12 groups: D2 at T = 64 and k = 16; D3 at k = 16 and T = 8, 64 and 256; C1 base b at 200 and 1000
      instructions and 25%; B3 at 1000 instructions, 25%, with every1, every8 and every32
      cuts; B4 re-association at n = 5 to 7), are never re-solved by a plain
      `check`: their solver-derived and timing-derived fields (the conflict counts, the
      verdict, the control, the proof size, `green`) are verified **only by `check --full`**,
      which is the release gate for the decision. A plain `check` prints
      `decision-critical groups not re-solved by this run: N`, where N is computed from what
      THIS run re-solved (round 3, finding 3): the decision groups with no quick-tier row and
      no row the spot check re-ran to completion. It is 12 when every cheap-class decision
      row was re-run (11 before VOS-D143 moved D2 at T = 64, k = 16 over the line), and larger
      on a host that sampled fewer rows (earlier measurements: 12 at `--jobs 2`, 17 with
      `--spot-budget 1`, both with the earlier table), so the printed figure is a lower bound on the gap, never a plan
      constant. What a plain `check` does verify of an expensive row is its **structure**
      (round 2 below).
  * **What the spot check proves, honestly (finding M3).** The first draft claimed an
    editor could not know in advance which rows would be re-run, because any edit reshuffles it.
    That is false as a defence: the seed is deterministic, an editor holds both the file and
