    this code, and so can compute the sample of a candidate file and **grind** it (change a
    byte somewhere harmless, such as a note) until the edited row falls outside the sample.
    The spot check catches an **accidental** drift (a row regenerated at another tree) and a
    **lazy** edit that does not grind. The sampling statement applies to the **cheap-class
    rows only**: for a single edit of a cheap row among the 500 eligible rows and a sample of
    16 to 300, an unprepared edit is caught with probability about `sample / population`. For
    an **expensive** row the probability that the CI spot check re-solves it is **0** (the plan
    never makes one eligible; this is what finding 1 of the second review found: an edit of
    10 rows in decision-critical expensive groups flipped `decide` to row 2 with `check: ok`).
    The structural rebuild below catches an edit of those rows that touches the instance's
    digest, size or entropy, for every comparable decision row that is not plan-heavy (round 3, finding 2: a row left
    unreached or unverified is a violation, not a count) and for a seeded, budgeted sample of
    the others; an edit of **only** solver-derived or timing
    fields of an expensive row (for example D3 at T = 8, k = 16: worst-step conflicts, solve
    time, DRAT size and `green`) is **not** caught by a plain `check`, whatever the sample. It
    is **not proof** of the file against a determined editor.
  * **The real teeth: `check --full` and a release gate.** `check --full` re-runs **every**
    full-tier row, no sampling, each row under the limits of item (3). (As built it ran
    locally, about 2 h on the reference Mac; VOS-028c3c-1 moves it to sharded CI runners,
    sections 9.2, 9.3 and 9.12.1.) It **refuses when a recorded tree id differs from the working tree**
    (and when the file records none: a file that predates tree ids is refused with that
    message) unless the rows are regenerated first, so it cannot pass on a file that no
    longer describes the tree. It also compares what drives `green` (finding M1): the
    recorded `solve_ms`, `worst_step_solve_ms`, `check_ms` and `peak_rss_kb` must each lie
    within a **two-sided** band of the re-run, `TIMING_BAND` = 4 (recorded at most 4 times
    the re-run's plus slack, and the re-run's at most 4 times the recorded plus slack;
    one-sided would let an editor lower a time to flip `green`), with absolute slack
    `TIMING_SLACK_MS` = 50 (times) and `RSS_SLACK_KB` = 8192, and `green` is **recomputed
    from the re-run's own measurements** and must equal the recorded one. A row that ended
    `killed`, `oom` or `crashed` has no reproducible time and is compared on its result
    only. `check --full` also compares the timing-derived fields of the in-process quick rows
    (round 2, finding 2) and requires the decision of the **re-run** rows to equal the
    recorded decision, in its row number and its `because` lines (the worst-solve figure, a
    time, is cut out; finding 3), which covers predicates outside `green` such as row 3's
    `solve_ms + check_ms <= 60 000`. So timing-derived verdicts are verified only by `check
    --full`, and `check --full` is
    the release gate: the VOS-028c3 decision stays **provisional** until a full re-run (`check
    --full --at-checkout` at the tree being released) has reproduced every full-tier row and the named reviewer
    of section 8.5 has signed that off. This is a release gate, not a CI step; on the
    committed file today it **refuses**, with the tree-id message and not a crash: the file's
    own provenance and its E1 refresh record no tree ids (they predate 0.2.0), and the
    three D095 re-record merges record the probe tree of 0f69ee86, which is not the tree
    `check --full` runs at once the hardening fixes are committed. It passes only after the
    rows are regenerated at the tree that is being released.
  **(2)** The kill checks the status of
  `/bin/kill`, retries it once, falls back to `child.kill()` and bounds the wait with a
  5 s `try_wait` loop (`runner::terminate`, tested with a stub child that ignores the kill). **(3) OS limits without `unsafe`, and no shell injection.** The child is
  launched through `/bin/sh` as a CPU backstop plus a kill-on-drop guard. The CPU
  limit N is a **validated unsigned integer computed from the row budget constant**
  (never read as text from the results file) and **formatted from a number**. The script
  string is the **fixed literal** `ulimit -t "$1" || exit 126; shift; exec "$@"`
  (a limit that cannot be set stops the row with status 126 instead of running it
  unbounded; finding L3); N, the program
  and its arguments are passed as **separate argv entries** (`sh -c <script> sh N
  program args...`), never joined into the script. A **negative control** passes a
  malicious knob and budget string (shell metacharacters such as `; touch x` and `$(...)`)
  and asserts that no file is created and that the string reaches the program only as an
  inert argument or is refused by `validate`. The RSS poll stays and `RLIMIT_AS` is
  still not used (9.5 item 4). **(4) Knob magnitudes are capped.** `validate` requires
  every row's `(experiment, mode, knob)` to be in the plan's closed set for the
  experiment, and checks the knob **values**, not only the names: every row's knob must
  **equal exactly** one of `knobs(exp)` (so a hand-edited length of 10^9 is refused,
  not run). The sampled re-run of item (1) runs **each row in a child process with the
  same resource limits as `run`** (CPU ulimit, RSS poll, kill), never in process.
  **(5)** Child output is read through `take(1 MiB)`,
  `--jobs` is clamped to 1..=64. **Files (findings L1 and L2).** `check`, `table`, `decide`
  and `merge` all read through one capped reader (refusing a file over 64 MiB, checked on
  the open file and enforced on the read). Every write (`run --out`, `merge --out`,
  `table --write`) goes through one atomic writer: a temporary file in the **same
  directory**, opened `create_new` (never following a planted file or link; mode 0600),
  named from the process id, a counter and the clock, written, `fsync`ed and renamed over
  the target, with the directory `fsync`ed; a target that is a symbolic link is refused;
  the result keeps the target's mode, or 0644. Process-group, `sh`
  and `ulimit` code is `cfg(unix)`: the Windows BUILD runs `check` in process only
  (its `validate` still applies; the sampled re-run is skipped there and says so).
* **Hardening, round 2 (second security review; tests in `tests/review_fixes2.rs`).**
  1. **Expensive rows (finding 1, MEDIUM).** (a) The statements above: a plain `check`
     re-solves only cheap-class rows; the expensive ones, decision-critical groups included,
     are verified only by `check --full`, the release gate; the sampling probability applies to
     cheap rows only. (b) **Structural rebuild.** Every full-tier row's instance is rebuilt in
     a child **without solving** (`vos-capacity-probe structure --spec`, under `run`'s limits;
     `exp::structure_of`, which mirrors `mono_row` and `chain_row`, pinned equal to `run_row` on
     the quick tier by a test), and its `instance_sha256`, `ands`, `vars`, `clauses`,
     `entropy_bits` and `declared_bits` are compared exactly (`compare_structure`). (The control
     digest needs the SAT search to find the control's site, so it is not rebuilt.) An
     enumeration row has no instance and is re-run and compared in full. Quick rows are
     already re-run in full in process. The rebuild visits the rows of the groups `decide`
     reads first, then the rest in the file-seeded order, skipping rows the spot check has
     already re-run, under a total budget `STRUCT_BUDGET` = 45 s with `STRUCT_SLACK` = 30 s per
     child (a child that does not finish is **not verified**, a failure, as in the spot
     check). A row whose recorded structure is partial (`killed`, `oom`, `crashed`, a chain
     that stopped on a cap or a SAT segment) is counted and left to `check --full`.
     **Cost, measured:** the whole population would take about 6 minutes of CPU (about 90 s at 4
     jobs), 320 s of it in the five D3 groups at T = 256 (one seed takes 52 s at k = 1 to 92 s at
     k = 16; every other group builds in at most 4.4 s). Those five groups are the plan's
     `cost::STRUCT_HEAVY` and are rebuilt only by `check --full`; the rest runs under the 45 s
     budget, so the structural pass is a **seeded sample for the non-decision rows** and covers
     the decision rows except the plan-heavy ones and those whose recorded structure is partial
     (the committed file: 85 decision rows, of which 80 are rebuilt or re-run, 3 are
     plan-heavy and 2 are partial; round 3 below makes the 80 mandatory). The structural line
     prints the counts. (c) `check` prints
     `decision-critical groups not re-solved: N`, the number of groups `results::decision_read`
     (which mirrors `decide`'s `find` calls; a test pins that it covers every group whose
     redness changes the decision of an all-green synthetic file) names that are in neither the
     quick tier nor `cost::CHEAP`.
  2. **`compare_rerun` compares every deterministic field (finding 2).** Exactly: experiment,
     knob, mode, tier, seed, seeds, the three digests, `ands`, `vars`, `clauses`,
     `entropy_bits`, `declared_bits`, `result`, `collapsed_reason`, `control`, `conflicts`,
     `propagations`, `worst_step_conflicts`, `steps`, `drat_bytes`, `check_work`, whether
     `check_ms` is present, `pass_default_limits`, `cap`, `note` and `wall_s`. The solver and
     the checker are deterministic (a test re-runs rows of four experiments twice and compares
     all of them; the 85 quick rows and the sampled rows of the committed file reproduce them
     on `check`). **Nondeterministic, compared only by `check --full`'s two-sided band:**
     `solve_ms`, `worst_step_solve_ms`, `check_ms` (wall times) and `peak_rss_kb`; `green` is a
     function of those and is recomputed. Metrics are compared as a **symmetric key set** (an
     extra or a missing key is a finding, including the recorded keys the old code never
     looked at) with exact values except `run_ms` and `cases_per_s`
     (`results::NONDETERMINISTIC_METRICS`, timings and rates). Under `--full` the in-process
     quick rows also go through `compare_timing` and the `green` recomputation (they borrow the
     recorded resident set, which they do not measure).
  3. **The re-run decision (finding 3).** `check --full` requires `decide(re-run rows)` to equal
     `decide(recorded rows)` in row number and `because` lines (`results::decision_differences`).
  4. **Canonical input (finding 4).** `check`, `table`, `decide` and `merge` (both inputs)
     refuse a file whose `to_text(from_text(text))` differs from the text
     (`Results::from_canonical_text`): a duplicate key (the parser keeps the first), an unknown
     key, a re-spaced field. The committed file round-trips exactly. Every narrowing conversion
     is a `try_from` that refuses on overflow (`seeds` was truncated from 64 to 32 bits).
  5. **Numeric bounds (finding 5).** `validate` requires every time to be finite and in 0 to
     `MAX_TIME_MS` = 3,600,000 (one hour: the longest budget is 600 s), `check_ms` finite or
     absent, and every counter under a bound with a stated reason (`MAX_CONFLICTS` = 2^40,
     `MAX_PROPAGATIONS` = 2^50, `MAX_SIZE` = 2^36 for ANDs, variables, clauses and entropy bits,
     `MAX_PROOF` = 2^40 for DRAT bytes and checker work, `MAX_STEPS` = 2^16, `MAX_RSS_KB` = 16
     GiB, `MAX_SEEDS` = 1000, metrics finite in 0 to 10^18), that `entropy_bits <=
     declared_bits`, that `worst_step_conflicts <= conflicts`, and that `seed` is in `1..=seeds`.
  6. **The cheap table cannot shrink quietly (finding 6).** `CHEAP` has at least 122 groups and
     at least 610 eligible rows (its current extent: 122 groups, 610 rows); a test pins both, and
     a second test pins the table to equal exactly the set the committed recording derives by
     the rule (VOS-D143), so shrinking the table is a visible change to this text and those tests. `SPOT_FLOOR` = 16 is a
     plain constant, and fewer than 16 eligible rows is a `check` failure.
  7. **Reader threads (finding 7).** The parent no longer `join`s a child's output readers: it
     waits for them with a shared deadline (`READER_WAIT` = 5 s, `recv_timeout`), detaches them
     on a timeout and ends the row `killed` (unverified). A failed group kill leaves an orphan
     holding the pipes; before this the wall bound was defeated. A test uses a child that leaves
     a background process holding both pipes.
  8. **The atomic writer (finding 8).** The finished file keeps `mode & 0o777` (setuid, setgid
     and sticky are dropped), and the target's name is cut to `TMP_STEM_MAX` = 150 bytes (on a
     character boundary) in the temporary name, which therefore stays under 255 bytes.
* **Hardening, round 3 (third security review; tests in `tests/review_fixes3.rs`).**
  **Owner decision, 2026-11-20:** the CI gap for the solver-derived and timing-derived fields
  of expensive rows is **accepted**. There is no scheduled or per-PR `check --full` job. The
  VOS-028c3 re-plan decision stays **provisional** until a `check --full` at one tree passes
  and a named reviewer signs off (the release gate above). **Superseded in part by
  VOS-028c3c-1 (2026-11-23):** a weekly, scheduled `check --full --at-checkout` now exists (section 9.3);
  the release gate itself is unchanged.
  1. **Collapse relabel (MEDIUM).** A red `unsat` row relabelled `"result":"collapsed",
     "collapsed_reason":"propagation-only"` passed a plain `check` and turned a decision group
     green (collapsed rows are excluded from every statistic; reproduction: B3 certified at
     1000 instructions, 25% divergence, every8 cuts, seed 2). Fixed without solving. (a)
     `validate` (`results::collapse_violations`): a reason needs `conflicts == 0 || clauses <=
     2`; `identical-structure` and `constant-fold` need `ands == 0`; a chain row collapses only
     as `identical-structure`; a collapsed row with no reason must carry one of the two G2
     notes of `exp::g2_entropy_note` and `exp::g2_ands_note` whose numbers match the row's own
     fields (`entropy_bits * 2 < declared_bits`, or `ands * 4 < full`). Every one of the 19
     collapsed rows of the committed file satisfies them (a test pins it). (b) The `structure`
     child reports the verdicts the rebuilt instance gives by itself
     (`Instance::structural_collapse_reason`, the entropy half rule, the 25% AND rule at
     divergence 100%, and for a chain `ands == 0` and the entropy half rule on the totals), and
     `compare_structure` requires a recorded `unsat` row not to collapse on rebuild and a
     recorded `collapsed` row to say exactly what the rebuild says. **What a rebuild cannot
     see** is a `propagation-only` collapse on a CNF of more than two clauses (zero conflicts
     is the solver's measurement); such a row on a single instance is refused by the rebuild
     and left to a re-solve (the spot check or `check --full`). A chain row cannot carry it at
     all (rule above), which covers D3 at T = 256, the decision rows the rebuild skips as
     plan-heavy. (c) `Instance::verify_collapse_reason` is now called, by the `structure`
     child on every structural reason, instead of guarding nothing.
  2. **Unreached decision rows (MEDIUM).** The structural pass has two budgets. The decision
     rows (`results::decision_read`) come first under `DECISION_STRUCT_BUDGET` = 150 s (the 80
     comparable, non-heavy decision rows take about 75 s on one job on the reference host,
     Apple silicon, release build), and every comparable, non-`STRUCT_HEAVY` decision row that
     is not reached or not verified is a **violation naming its identity**. The others keep
     `STRUCT_BUDGET` = 45 s and are counted if not reached. Before this, `check
     --jobs 1 --spot-budget 1` printed `decision rows verified 50 of 85` and passed; it now
     verifies 80 of 85 (3 plan-heavy and 2 partial rows are named in the line) or fails. A
     host more than twice as slow as the reference at `--jobs 1` fails loudly. Measured
     wall time of a plain `check` on the committed file (14-core reference host): 2 min 15 s
     at the default jobs, 2 min 24 s at `--jobs 2`.
  3. **Overclaims.** The count of decision groups not re-solved follows the run (finding 1(c)
     text above); the sampling statements say what is verified for which rows: the spot check
     re-solves only the cheap-class rows it reaches inside its budget, so the number of rows is
     host-dependent; the structural rebuild is mandatory for the decision rows except the five
     plan-heavy groups, and a sample for the rest.
  4. **`structure_comparable`** returned true for a chain row that ended `sat`, though such a
     row built only the segments up to the counterexample; it now returns false.
* E1 rows are untouched (VOS-D103).
* **As built, the constants and the choices the text above left open.** The spot check's
  total budget is **60 s** (`spot::SPOT_BUDGET`; `check --spot-budget SECS` shortens it for
  tests and local runs, up to 600 s, and the floor still applies), the slack of a child's
  hard limit **15 s** (`SPOT_SLACK`) and the floor of verified rows **16** (`SPOT_FLOOR`).
  The cost class is `cost::CHEAP`, 122 of the 400 full-tier non-E knob groups (610 of the
  2,000 rows), those whose slowest seed took at most 3 s (`CHEAP_MAX_MS` = 3000) in the
  committed CI recording (run 45486250172; a `killed` or `oom` seed counts as infinitely slow);
  `cost::derive_cheap` is that rule, and a test fails until the table equals exactly the set it
  derives from the committed file, so a re-record that moves a group changes the table in the
  same commit. (VOS-028c3c-3b-1c re-derived the table from the CI recording: it had been derived
  from the Mac recording and lacked the B3 every2/every4 cuts and D5 groups, and an exact test had
  been loosened to "at most 4x the line" in commit B.) The seed is the SHA-256 of
  `Results::to_text()` (the canonical serialisation; the committed file is canonical, so
  it equals the SHA-256 of the file's bytes, which a test pins); the order sorts rows by
  SHA-256 of `seed:index`. The population excludes quick-tier rows (they are re-run in
  process by `check`, as before) and E1 and E2. `skipped` and `not reached`
  count the rows of the seeded order up to where the budget ended. A sampled row whose
  `(experiment, mode, knob)` is not in the plan is refused and not run. The `stale:`
  comparison uses `git rev-parse HEAD:<path>`, with `+uncommitted` appended when
  `git status --porcelain -- <path>` is not empty, so an edited working tree is stale
  against any record. `check --full` also refuses when a `merges` entry records no tree
  ids or different ones, because rows merged from another tree do not describe this
  one; it re-runs the quick rows in process as well. N of the `ulimit` wrapper is the
  hard limit in whole seconds (`runner::cpu_limit_s`: 300 for a 120 s row, 780 for a
  600 s chain), and a `GroupGuard` kills the child's process group if the parent
  unwinds. The sampled and full re-runs call `runner::run_one_bounded` (`run_one` with a
  tighter hard limit), the function `run` uses.

**VOS-028c3: the experiments and the decision (split into `c3a`, `c3b`, `c3c`).** The experiments of 8.6.3, in
`src/sweep.rs` and `tests/sweep.rs` with edits to `exp.rs`, `shapes.rs`, `prog.rs`,
`results.rs` and `table.rs`. Because `plan(Tier::Full)` and `vos30-capacity-results.json`
must stay in sync (`FULL_PLAN_EXEMPT` stays empty) and full recording runs on Unix:

* **`VOS-028c3a` (done, #19795):** B3 `every2`/`every4` support, D5 (touched-word obligations,
  separate structural frame obligation, two-mutant G3), `route_green` (8.6.1), `cut_density_k`
  (8.6.2) and `decide_replan` (8.6.4, including the orthogonal memory verdict), with a
  negative control for every clause in `tests/results_logic.rs` and shape/G3 tests in
  `tests/shapes.rs`; `plan(Tier::Full)` is not expanded yet.
* **`VOS-028c3b` (this step):** B5 (`src/sweep.rs` and `tests/sweep.rs`), G3 unswept-control
  replay and signature-merge negative control, and `B5 fuse = 1` `instance_sha256` parity
  with B2; `plan(Tier::Full)` is not expanded yet.
* **`VOS-028c3c` (planned):** Register B3 `every2`/`every4`, D5 and B5 in `plan(Tier::Full)`,
  enable `table --check` for **`**Re-plan decision (VOS-028c):**`** of 8.6 (distinct from 8.5's
  label because 8.5 scans up to the next level-two heading, a range that contains 8.6),
  record wholesale on CI runners (section 9.12.1: sharded `run`, `join`, `table --write`,
  `decide`; the results in a second commit that touches only `code/specs/**`), and
  fill 8.6's decision sentence, the README's "What it found", the backlog (fund or re-plan
  VOS-029a and b; the budget of VOS-040) and VOS00 section 10.2. **As built (commit B):** the
  recording, the table, the 8.3 decision and the 8.6 decision sentence are done; `table --check`
  was **not** extended to the 8.6 label, because no subcommand prints `decide_replan` and adding
  one would change the probe's `src/` and so stale the recording (backlog VOS-D142).
  **As built (VOS-028c3c-3b-1c, probe 0.2.0):** `decide --replan` prints the label, K, the memory
  verdict and the `because` lines; `table --write` splices them into the replan block of 8.6 and
  `table --check` compares that block and the hand-written label (above). It changes the probe's
  `src/`, so the recording is stale by tree id; the verify-at-the-checkout run of VOS-D141 is
  what re-anchors it. The computed answer on the CI recording (run 45486250172) agrees with the
  sentence that was hand-written in commit B: R3, K = 2, shipped T. The same step re-derives
  `cost::CHEAP` from that recording (VOS-D143, see 9.12 item 6 and the cost-class paragraph).
* B5 and D5 are validated by guards G1 to G3 as every experiment is; the extra G3
  conditions are in 8.6.3. B5's `fuse = 1` `instance_sha256` equals B2's.
* **Decision procedure for 8.6.** `decide_replan` classifies the recorded rows by the
  table of 8.6.4 and returns the row, the condition that placed it and the memory
  verdict. A group is green iff at least one row is counted and every counted row is
  route-green; a missing group is red.

#### 9.12.1 Pre-registration: the recording and the full verify on CI runners (VOS-028c3c-1)

**Status: specification, pre-registered before any code or recording exists.** The
code is VOS-028c3c-2a (comparison and `validate`), 2b (shards, `join`, provenance) and 2c
(`sign`; all built), then VOS-028c3c-3 (the workflow); the recording is
VOS-028c3c and the sign-off VOS-028c3d. The host, `--jobs`, N and cadence are fixed in
sections 9.2 and 9.3; this subsection fixes the comparison rule for wall-capped rows,
the recording and the rule for discarding one. As in 8.6, a change to any of these after
a recording exists is a change to this specification and a re-record, never a tuning.

**Amended by VOS-028c3c-3b (VOS-D141) after recording 45486250172 and before any verify has executed:** a verify runs at the checkout's tree, which may differ from the recorded tree. This changes no comparison class, band, slack, retry rule, discard rule or decision table, and it was decided with no verify outcome in hand, so it is not a tuning; it changes nothing about how a recording is made, so it requires no re-record.

**The comparison rule for wall-capped rows (new; implemented in VOS-028c3c-2,
VOS-D124).**

* **The defect.** `results::compare_rerun` compares `conflicts`, `propagations`, `steps`,
  `drat_bytes` and every other deterministic field exactly, with no carve-out for a row
  whose `cap` is `wall`. `results::compare_timing` already exempts rows that ended
  `killed`, `oom` or `crashed` (they have no reproducible time), but it is a timing
  comparison and does not reach the counters. A wall-capped row's counters are whatever
  was done when the clock ran out, so they differ between any two runs. In the
  committed file, B2 `{length: 500, divergence: 1.0}` seeds 1 and 2 are `unknown` on the
  wall at 860,024 and 740,026 conflicts. **141 of the 1,625 committed rows are
  `unknown` with cap `wall`** (counted from the file; another 122 are `unknown` with cap
  `conflicts`, which is deterministic and unaffected). With the rule as it stands, `check
  --full` could never pass on an honest recording.
* **The rule (amended in VOS-028c3c-2a, VOS-D128).** A row recorded, or re-run, with cap
  `wall` falls into one of two reduced classes. In both, the counters, proof fields,
  timing-band fields, `steps`, `note`, the control fields and the metrics are excluded,
  as for `killed`.
  * **A wall-capped single instance** (the experiment is not segmented, and both rows are
    `unknown`) is compared on its identity `(experiment, knob, mode, tier, seed, seeds)`,
    its **instance structure fields** (`instance_sha256`, `ands`, `vars`, `clauses`,
    `entropy_bits`, `declared_bits`), its `result`, its `cap` and `wall_s`. The structure
    fields stay because a single instance, B5's unswept miter included, is built whole
    before the solve starts, so they do not depend on where the clock ran out; dropping
    them would let an edited instance hide behind a timeout.
  * **A wall-capped segmented row** (`exp::is_segmented`: the chain experiments other
    than B5) is compared on its identity, `result`, `cap` and `wall_s` only. The chain
    row hashes only the segments it reached before the deadline, so its instance digest
    and its structure fields (`ands`, `vars`, `clauses`, `steps`, `note`) are partial and
    differ between any two runs. Counted from the committed file, **64 of the 141
    wall-capped rows are segmented** (D4 55, B3 7, D3 2); the other 77 (D1 37, B2 20, C1
    20) are single instances.
  * **A row that ended `killed`, `oom` or `crashed`** on either side is compared on its
    identity, `result`, `cap` and `wall_s` only: its digests are `pending` or
    `unavailable` and its counters are partial (`oom` has cap `rss`, so no other-cap rule
    could ever reproduce it). The committed file has none of the three (counted from the
    file; its results are `unsat` 1,208, `unknown` 263, `pass` 75, `refused` 60,
    `collapsed` 19).
  A flip between a completed verdict (`unsat`, `collapsed`, `pass`) and `unknown` with cap
  `wall` is a **finding** (one `result differs` line), handled by the re-run rule below.
  Rows with any other cap, including `unknown` with cap `conflicts`, are compared exactly
  as today. `compare_timing` (the band) is skipped when either row is in a reduced class.
* **The re-run wall (VOS-D131).** The in-process re-run of a quick-tier row uses that
  row's own experiment wall (`row_wall_s`: 600 s for a chain row, 1,800 s for B5), not a
  flat 120 s, so a row that was recorded with a 600 s clock is re-run with one.
* **The re-run rule.** A verify shard that fails is re-run **once**, on the same runner
  class with the same binary. If the re-run passes, the shard is logged in the
  tracking issue as a flake, **by shard index only** (amended by VOS-028c3c-3a, so the
  issue text stays fixed strings plus indices and never carries row values).
  **The retry is a separate job** (`verify-retry`, VOS-028c3c-3b), not a second attempt
  inside the shard step: one shard's absolute bound is 285 minutes (section 9.2), so two
  attempts are 570 minutes against a `timeout-minutes` of 330. A GitHub "re-run failed
  jobs" attempt of a verify run is **not** the sanctioned retry (it changes the run
  attempt and the exact-set check of 9.12.2 item 2 refuses it); a fresh dispatch is. A row that fails twice keeps the workflow red until it is
  investigated or the file is wholesale re-recorded. `TIMING_BAND` (4, two-sided, in
  `results.rs`) and the slacks (`TIMING_SLACK_MS` = 50, `RSS_SLACK_KB` = 8192) are never
  changed in response to a failure.
* **As built (VOS-028c3c-2b-ii).** The mechanics of the rule are `check --full --shard
  K/N`, which exits 1 on a difference and still writes its `--rerun-out` file, so a
  shard that is re-run once (above) produces a second file for the same shard; `join`
  takes one file per shard and refuses a duplicate, so the workflow chooses which file
  represents the shard. The decision is then checked once, by `decision-diff` of the
  recorded file against the joined verify file (section 9.2); a difference there is a
  finding of the second class below, never a reason to discard.
* **Verify at the checkout (VOS-D141, VOS-028c3c-3b-1a).** `check --full --at-checkout` re-runs every row with a binary built from the checkout and compares it with the committed file under the classes above. Inputs whose ids differ from the recorded `trees` are printed as `note:` lines, not refused. A file with no tree ids (or only legacy whole-crate names), a plan-coverage gap or an uncommitted checkout (a `+uncommitted` id) is still refused. The flag is valid only with `--full`; without it `check --full` is unchanged and a stale input still refuses. Green means the recorded rows reproduce at the checkout's tree. Any difference is a finding: the edit is fixed or the file is wholesale re-recorded; rows and recorded ids are never edited. The file's own `tool.trees` never change, because they record where it was recorded. A verify-mode file carries `recording.verifies_sha256`, the SHA-256 of the recorded file rendered without its signing banner (so the value is the same before and after `sign`); `join` requires it equal across shards, and `decision-diff` requires it to equal the unsigned digest of `--recorded` in place of the former same-tree-ids refusal. `decision-diff` compares every identity's row with `results::compare_verify`, the pure comparison that `check --full` and the spot check also use, and prints row differences and decision differences in separate labelled sections; it exits 0 only when both are empty. `decision-diff` also refuses a verify file whose own `trees` are not those of a committed checkout (none, the legacy whole-crate names, an `+uncommitted` id or a malformed id) or that records merges, the same conditions under which `check --full --at-checkout` refuses to produce one.

**The recording is a wholesale re-record of every row at one tree.** Why, disclosed:

* The committed file is tool 0.1.0 and its `tool` block records no tree ids (nor does
  its E1 merge; the three D095 merges record the trees of commit 0f69ee86), so
  `check --full` refuses it (`full_refusals`: "the file records no tree ids"). It was
  recorded on `aarch64-apple-darwin` (the file's `host`), not on the runner class that
  will verify it.
* `merge --add` cannot repair that: it keeps the base `tool`, `host` and `revisions` as
  the file's provenance and appends only new rows, so the file would still have no tree
  ids in its `tool` block and would still be refused.
* The verdicts of wall-limited rows depend on the host. A row that was `unsat` on the
  Mac may record `unknown` on the runner, and the other way round. **The decision is
  whatever the runner recording gives**; no row is kept from the Mac file to keep a
  verdict.
* The host class changes timings, so the file is recorded and verified on the same
  runner class (section 9.2).
* The post-record commit (commit B of VOS-028c3c) may touch only `code/specs/**`. (As first
  designed the probe's recorded tree id covered its whole directory, so any other edit
  after the recording would stale it. VOS-D139 and VOS-D140 narrowed the recorded ids to
  the build inputs of section 9.2, so a README, CHANGELOG or test edit no longer stales a
  recording; an edit of `src/`, a manifest, the lockfile or an included file still does, and
  the rule stays because it is simple and the guard enforces it.) Why the narrowing: the
  recording of run 45417745745 at 47fad32 could not land, because a probe test asserted the
  pre-recording state (D139) and any fix of it changed the whole-directory tree (D140). **The control is
  per commit (VOS-D126, amended by VOS-028c3c-3a):** from the last commit that touches
  the results file onward, every commit touches only `code/specs/**`. A rule over a whole
  PR would fail the very PR it protects, because VOS-028c3c puts commit A (the plan
  activation) and commit B (the recorded file) in one PR. It runs as a script from
  `ci.yml`'s metadata-contracts step, not in the record workflow, because
  `pull_request` is forbidden there (9.12.2 item 4).

  **As built (VOS-D126):** `code/scripts/vos_results_guard.py --base <sha> --head <sha>`,
  with its test `code/scripts/tests/test_vos_results_guard.py`, both run from
  the metadata-contracts job of `ci.yml` (the script on `pull_request` events only, with
  the PR's base and head SHAs passed through `env:`). The rule: take the PR's commits on
  the first-parent chain, oldest first; find the last commit that changes
  `code/specs/data/vos30-capacity-results.json`; that commit and every later one may change
  only paths under `code/specs/**`. A commit that changes the results file together with
  any path outside `code/specs/**` fails for the same reason. If the range does not touch
  the results file the guard passes at once. **Merge commits** (a `git merge origin/main`
  into the branch) count only the paths they change relative to *both* parents, which is
  the conflict-resolution content; a path that the merge took unchanged from the other
  parent is not the PR's own edit and is ignored (a merge that brings in a newer results
  file from main is likewise not "the commit that changes the results file"). Exit codes:
  0 pass, 1 violation, 2 usage or git error (a missing or malformed SHA, an unknown
  object): the CI step fails closed. The guard does not run on a push to `main`: the repository
  squash-merges, so the pushed commit holds commit A and commit B together and the
  per-commit rule cannot hold there; the gate is the PR, and `check --full` still refuses
  a results file whose recorded input ids differ from the merged tree.
  **Rule 2:** (in a PR whose range changes the results file, deletion included, after rule 1's merge discounting) because rule 1 cannot see the recorded trees going stale through main (a merge from main takes its paths unchanged, and a squash lands on a newer main), the same step also requires that every input id the file records (`tool.trees`, one per entry of section 9.2) equals `git rev-parse HEAD:<path>` for its path at the checked-out merge ref (`--tree-rev`, default `HEAD`); a file with no trees passes with a note, a mismatch exits 1 naming the path, and malformed input exits 2. Rule 1 discounts a merge's non-first parent only when that parent is already contained in the PR's base (a merge of a side branch of the PR itself is judged by its full diff), and a results file with a `recording` block must record exactly the entries of section 9.2 (omitting them cannot switch rule 2 off; the guard's name-to-path map is tested equal to `TREE_ENTRIES`, VOS-D140). That a PR can edit `ci.yml` or the guard itself, with no CODEOWNERS, is covered by the release gate G1 (CODEOWNERS and branch protection, VOS-D026 and VOS-D067), not by this guard. Rule 2 and the ratchet are scoped to PRs that change the results file because a required PR gate on tree equality would fail every PR touching the probe or the four shared solver crates once a recording exists and freeze unrelated work until a full re-record; tree drift on main is the job of the weekly verify of 9.3 (it re-runs the rows at main's tree; a red result means the edit is fixed or a re-record follows). A ratchet also refuses to downgrade a recorded file: if the results file at the PR base records trees or has a `recording` block, the PR's file must hold all five trees, and deleting it fails (it needs a spec change). CI passes `--tree-rev` as `github.sha`, the merge commit on a pull request. **Amended by VOS-028c3c-3b (VOS-D141):** a signed file passes when either its recorded `trees` or its banner's `verified.trees` equal the merge ref's ids (VOS-028c3c-3b-1b: `regeneration.verified.trees` is validated exactly as strictly as `tool.trees`, the exact entry set and 40-hex ids, and a malformed one exits 2; the ratchet and rule 1 are unchanged). A non-null `regeneration` must carry a well-formed banner or the guard exits 2: `unsigned_sha256` and `verified.verify_sha256` 64 lowercase hex digits, `verified.head_sha` 40 lowercase hex digits, `verified.run_id` and `verified.run_attempt` 1 to 20 digits, and a `verified` block with its `trees`, so a signed file without `verified` (which the probe also refuses) fails closed; every id pattern is matched in full, so a trailing newline never passes).

**The discard rule.** A recording is discarded **only for an infrastructure failure,
never for row values.** The whole recording is discarded (there is no partial
salvage: shard membership depends on N), it is logged with the workflow run id and the
condition **before** any re-dispatch, and **the row artifacts of a discarded recording
are never opened.**

* **Infrastructure failures** (the complete list):
  1. the build job fails;
  2. a runner is lost, or the job is cancelled by GitHub;
  3. a shard hits its `timeout-minutes` or the 6 h job limit;
  4. the binary artifact download, or a shard's artifact upload, fails, or a shard's
     recomputed digest of the binary differs from the build job's (section 9.12.2, item 1);
  5. the probe exits with status 2. In this code that is `Failure::Usage`: a malformed
     command line, or an input that cannot be read or an output that cannot be
     written (`main.rs`; status 1 is `Failure::Violations`). `run --tier full` returns
     `Failure::Violations` nowhere (only the quick-tier gate does), so a status 1 from a
     full-tier `run` does not occur, and a status 1 from `check` or from `validate` is
     a finding about rows (the second class below), never an infrastructure failure;
  6. a shard's output is missing, truncated, or fails `validate FILE` with exit status 2
     (a structural finding; the `validate` subcommand, VOS-D130, exits 2 on a
     structural finding, 1 on row findings only and 0 when clean);
  7. `join` refuses: a digest, tree-id or revision mismatch, rows that are not disjoint,
     a union that is not equal to the plan, or a refusal under `--expect-run-id` or
     `--expect-binary-sha256` (section 9.12.2, items 1 and 2);
  8. `/usr/bin/time`, `ps` or `ulimit` is unavailable on a runner.
* **Three classes of finding (VOS-D129, decided by the coordinator before any
  recording exists, so a pre-registration and not a tuning).**
  1. **Structural** (`validate FILE` exit 2: no rows, a dirty tree, missing tree ids, an
     open-set value, a bad `wall_s`, a wrong seed count, and the infrastructure list
     above): the recording is **discarded**.
  2. **Row-guard** (`validate FILE` exit 1: any `rows`-class finding, for example a
     failed control, a red `green`, a counter or RSS violation, a collapse or B5-twin
     digest mismatch): the recording is **kept**. It blocks commit B of VOS-028c3c and
     opens an issue; the resolution is an investigation followed by a wholesale
     re-record or a change to this specification, never an edit of rows.
  3. **Clean** (exit 0): committable.
* **Row outcomes are always kept:** `unsat`, `sat`, `unknown` (any cap), `collapsed`,
  `refused`, `killed`, `oom`, `crashed`, a red `green`, a failed control and any
  `decide` outcome. Kept does not mean committable: a row outcome that is a row-guard
  finding (class 2 above) is kept and blocks commit B. On a **verify**, a
  `TIMING_BAND` miss or a difference in a deterministic field is a **finding**, handled
  by the re-run rule above; it is never a reason to discard a recording.
* **On a shard timeout:** re-record wholesale at **N' = 2N = 128**, with the same
  `--jobs`, without inspecting values. A second timeout stops the recording and opens an
  issue. A partial salvage is impossible, because shard membership changes with N.

**`binary_sha256`: decided (VOS-D125).** What the code does today: `validate` requires only
that `binary_sha256` be 64 hex digits, `join` is planned to refuse unless it is identical
across shards, and `check --full` compares the tree ids (`trees`) of the file with the
working tree and does **not** compare `binary_sha256` with the verifying binary. A verify
build from the checkout's tree on the same runner class need not be byte-identical. The decision:

* **Within one workflow run**, every shard and `join` must match the build job's digest
  (section 9.12.2, items 1 and 2).
* **Between a recording and a later verify**, `binary_sha256` is **informational**. A
  verify builds the binary afresh from the checkout's tree and is not required to be
  byte-identical, so a green verify is evidence that the tree reproduces the recorded
  rows under the comparison rules, **not** that the two binaries are identical. No
  reviewer reads it as binary equivalence.
* **No out-of-tool tree-id computation is required.** The tree ids `check --full` compares
  are computed by the reviewed probe, built from the checked-out tree in the same run,
  and the binary is pinned by item 1 of section 9.12.2 within that run.

**As built (VOS-028c3c-3a): `.github/workflows/vos-capacity-full.yml`, part a.** Triggers:
`workflow_dispatch` only, with an input `mode` whose only choice is `record`; there is no
`schedule` and no verify job yet. Jobs: `gate` (in this part it only sets `mode` from the
dispatch input; there will be no Actions-API gate: the verify is weekly and on dispatch, VOS-D141), `build` (one binary at
`$RUNNER_TEMP/target`, its SHA-256 and the workspace path as job outputs, uploaded with 5
retention days), `record-shard` (64 legs, `max-parallel: 8`, `fail-fast: false`; each
checks out the same commit at the same workspace path, rechecks the binary's digest,
runs `run --tier full --shard K/64 --jobs 2` and then `validate`), `record-join`
(download-all plus the exact-set check, `join` with all five `--expect-*` flags, the joined
file kept 90 days with its SHA-256 in the step summary, then `validate`; nothing is
pushed) and `report` (the only job with `issues: write`). The structural test is
`code/scripts/tests/test_vos_capacity_full_workflow.py`, run from `ci.yml`. Part b (the
verify path, the gate and the schedule, VOS-028c3c-3b) follows the recording
(VOS-028c3c), because the committed results file records no tree ids and every
scheduled verify before then would be red.

**As built (VOS-028c3c-3b-2): `.github/workflows/vos-capacity-full.yml`, part b, the verify path.**
Triggers are exactly `workflow_dispatch` (input `mode`, a choice of `record` or `verify`, default
`verify`) and `schedule` with ONE cron, `41 1 * * 0` (Sundays 01:41 UTC, off `ci.yml`'s slots), which
runs a verify unconditionally. There is no gate beyond the existing one, no baseline, no key, no
Actions-API lookup and no `actions: read` (owner decision "Weekly only", 2026-11-24). The jobs, each
with `contents: read` only (the failure job has `issues: write` and nothing else), a
`timeout-minutes` on the job and on every step, and every action pinned by SHA:

* `gate` sets `mode` to `verify` on `schedule`, to the input on `workflow_dispatch`, and fails on any
  other event or any other mode; record mode can never come from `schedule`. It still refuses
  another repository and a non-branch ref.
* `build` is shared with record and unchanged.
* `verify-shard` (64 legs, `max-parallel: 8`, `fail-fast: false`, job timeout 330 and the probe step 320
  minutes, as for the record shard) checks out the same commit at the same workspace path, rechecks
  the binary's digest, and runs `check --full --at-checkout --shard K/64 --rerun-out F --run-id I
  --run-attempt A --exe BINARY --jobs 2 FILE` against the committed results file in ITS checkout.
  Exit 0, or exit 1 with a non-empty re-run file: it uploads the file and an `exit-code` file
  (`verify-shard-K-of-64-<run_id>-<run_attempt>`) and the job succeeds, because a difference is a
  finding that the retry and the fan-in judge. Exit 2, or exit 1 with no re-run file: the job fails
  and nothing retries it (a structural finding or an infrastructure failure, 9.12.1).
* `verify-triage` (`!cancelled()` and `mode == 'verify'`) downloads every artifact of the run, requires
  exactly the 64 shard artifacts and the binary, validates each `exit-code` strictly (one `0` or `1`
  and a newline at most; it is data from an artifact, so it is never expanded as shell) and outputs
  the JSON list of the K whose exit code is 1.
* `verify-retry` is a dynamic matrix over that list, skipped when it is empty. It repeats the
  shard's checkout, workspace and digest steps and runs the same command once, uploading
  `...-retry`. It is a separate job (9.12.1), so a retry never shares a timeout with a first
  attempt. A retry that fails like a shard (exit 2, or exit 1 with no file) fails the job.
* `verify-fan-in` has its OWN checkout of the run's commit, asserts `HEAD` is `GITHUB_SHA` and the
  committed results file is unmodified, downloads everything, and requires the artifact set to be
  exactly the binary, the 64 shard artifacts and the retries the triage listed. A retried shard's
  `-retry` file replaces its first file. It runs `join` with all five `--expect-*` flags, uploads the
  joined verify file (90 days) and writes its SHA-256, the commit, the ref and the run id to the
  step summary, then runs `decision-diff --recorded <the committed file in its checkout> --rerun
  <joined> --expect-shards 64`. The upload precedes `decision-diff` on purpose, as
  `record-join` uploads before `validate`: a red verify keeps its file so the differences can be
  read, and `sign` refuses any file that is not clean. It outputs the indices of the shards whose
  retry exited 0 (the flakes).
* `verify-verdict` (`always()` and `mode == 'verify'`) fails unless `verify-fan-in` concluded
  `success`; it is the definition of "green" for a verify (8.5). There is no `continue-on-error`
  anywhere, so a shard that fails, a retry that fails, a missing artifact and a difference all end
  in a red verdict.
* `report` now also covers verify. Its text is fixed strings, the run id, the run URL, the mode, the
  job results and shard INDEX lists only (re-checked against a digit-and-comma pattern before use),
  never row values or author-controlled text. A failed or cancelled run comments on the "a run
  failed" issue; a green run whose retry rescued a shard comments on a separate "verify flakes"
  issue (the flake log of 9.12.1).

**How 3b-2 is proven (owner decision, 2026-11-25).** Asked how, the owner answered "Skip branch run; verify on main (Recommended)". There is no pre-merge branch dispatch: each verify costs about 30 to 60 runner-hours, a branch verify never moves the standing of the decision (9.12.2 item 5), and the main verify is needed for VOS-028c3d anyway. 3b-2 merges after CI and the security review; the first verify is then dispatched on main, which both proves the workflow and is the evidence VOS-028c3d signs. A workflow bug that run exposes gets a fix PR and a re-dispatch on main.

**Closure of VOS-D144 (a).** `decision-diff --recorded` is the committed results file read from the
`verify-fan-in` job's own checkout of the run's commit, never from an artifact, so the digest that
`verifies_sha256` is checked against is the committed file's and not something the verify file
asserts about itself; no `--expect-recorded-sha256` flag is needed. The verify shards likewise
read the committed file from their own checkouts, at the commit the run named.

#### 9.12.2 Workflow and provenance security (pre-registered; implemented in VOS-028c3c-2 and VOS-028c3c-3)

**Status: requirements, from the security review of VOS-028c3c-1 (VOS-D125).** The probe
items are VOS-028c3c-2 and the workflow items are VOS-028c3c-3. As in 9.12.1, a change to
one after a recording exists is a change to this specification and a re-record.

1. **The shared binary.** The build job computes the SHA-256 of the binary and exposes it
   as a job output. Every shard downloads the artifact, recomputes the digest and fails
   if it differs (an infrastructure failure, discard list item 4 of 9.12.1), before any
   use of `--exe`. `join` takes `--expect-binary-sha256 <hex>` and refuses unless every
   shard's recorded `binary_sha256` equals it. The artifact is uploaded with
   `if-no-files-found: error` and a `retention-days` of **at least 5** (amended by
   VOS-028c3c-3a: at `max-parallel: 8` the last shard can start about 27 hours after the
   build, and the retry and fan-in jobs come later still, so a one-day artifact could
   expire under a run that is still using it).
2. **Shard outputs.** Shard artifacts are named with the shard index, N, the run id and the
   run attempt (`shard-<K>-of-<N>-<run_id>-<run_attempt>`). The join job downloads
   **all** of the run's artifacts with one `actions/download-artifact` step that has no
   `name`, `pattern`, `run-id` or `github-token` (so it can only see this run's), and a
   script step then requires the downloaded directory set to equal **exactly** the N
   expected shard names plus the binary artifact's name (plus, in the verify path of
   VOS-028c3c-3b, the retry names the triage lists), failing on anything missing or extra.
   (Amended by VOS-028c3c-3a; this replaces "downloads exactly N artifacts by exact name".
   Downloading N names one by one cannot detect an extra artifact, and N literal download
   steps would be the only way to follow the old wording.) A GitHub "re-run failed jobs"
   changes the run attempt, so the set check refuses it: that is the L1 rule for record
   mode, and for a verify it means a fresh dispatch. Each shard's output records the workflow run id (a provenance field
   VOS-028c3c-2 adds) and `join` takes `--expect-run-id` and refuses a shard whose run id
   differs.
3. **`binary_sha256` between runs.** Decided in 9.12.1: pinned within a run by items 1 and
   2, informational between a recording and a later verify.
4. **Triggers.** The workflow's triggers are exactly `workflow_dispatch` (record mode and an
   on-demand verify) and `schedule` (the weekly verify). `pull_request`,
   `pull_request_target` and `workflow_run` are forbidden. Record mode runs only on
   a branch of this repository: a job-level `if:` on the literal repository name
   (`adhithyan15/coding-adventures`) and `startsWith(github.ref, 'refs/heads/')`, which
   excludes tags (amended by VOS-028c3c-3a; the earlier wording named `refs/heads/main`
   or a branch, and a tag ref is neither). The same check also runs in the gate, whose
   step fails loudly on another repository or a non-branch ref, so a tag or fork
   dispatch is a red run and not a silent skip. The failure job's issue title and body are
   built from fixed strings plus the run id and run URL, never from row values, branch
   names, commit messages or other text an author controls.
5. **No gate; the baseline is a run on `main` (amended by VOS-028c3c-3b, VOS-D141; owner decision "Weekly only", 2026-11-24).** There is no gated run: the verify runs weekly unconditionally and on dispatch (section 9.3), so the gate of VOS-028c3c-3a's draft (a lookup of the last green run, a skip on equal keys, a skip after a red run) is not built and its fail-closed clauses fall away. What remains of a baseline is the reading of 8.5: the standing of the decision follows the most recent executed `verify-verdict` on `main`. A verify dispatched on another branch never moves that standing. Dispatch and schedule share one concurrency group (`cancel-in-progress: false`, section 9.3).
6. **No shared caches.** The build job restores no `actions/cache` entry from a key that a
   `pull_request` run of any workflow can write. If a Cargo cache is used it is keyed to
   this workflow and written only from `main`. The binary is built in a clean target
   directory.
7. **Recording runs are listed by GitHub, not by an editable note.** The Actions run
   history of `vos-capacity-full.yml` is the list of record-mode dispatches. The
   `regeneration` banner that `sign` writes (VOS-D120) records the run id, the run
   attempt and the head revision of the recording, and the digest of the unsigned file. The reviewer who signs (VOS-028c3d) lists every record-mode run at the
   recorded tree from the Actions history and confirms that each one other than the
   signed run was discarded under the discard rule of 9.12.1, with its log entry. A
   record-mode run at the same tree that is neither signed nor logged as discarded makes
   the recording unsignable; this stops a recording being picked from several. Discard
   classification is made from job conclusions and log lines, never from row artifacts
   (9.12.1). **Provenance of the signed run (amended by VOS-028c3c-3a after its
   security review).** A `workflow_dispatch` runs the workflow file of the dispatched
   ref, and any `refs/heads/*` branch qualifies, so the run list in the Actions history
   includes runs on any branch, and a run of a modified workflow or probe yields a
   joined artifact that looks like a real one. The join job therefore writes the
   commit (`GITHUB_SHA`), the ref, the run id and the run attempt to the step summary
   beside the digest, and the signer, before signing, confirms (a) that the run's commit
   is the reviewed commit A of the VOS-028c3c PR (its input ids are what `sign`
   checks, VOS-D137) and (b) that `.github/workflows/vos-capacity-full.yml` at that
   commit is byte-identical to main's (`git diff <sha> origin/main --
   .github/workflows/vos-capacity-full.yml` is empty). Otherwise the recording is not
   signed. **`sign` also takes the joined verify file and its SHA-256 (amended by VOS-028c3c-3b, VOS-D141; built in 3b-1b as `--verify-file J --expect-verify-sha256 V`, section 9.2):** it checks that the file verifies this very recorded file (`verifies_sha256`), at the working tree, with no row or decision difference, and records `verified` (run id, attempt, commit, digest, tree ids) in the banner. The reviewer takes `V` from the verify run's step summary, as for the recording's digest, and confirms, as for the recording, that the verify run's commit and workflow file are what is on main.
8. **Permissions.** Top-level `permissions: contents: read`; each job lists only what it
   needs and never widens beyond it. The build, shard and join jobs have `contents: read`
   only. The gate job has `contents: read` only: with no gated run (item 5) it reads no Actions API and needs no `actions: read`. The `verify-verdict` job is the definition of "green" for a verify (amended by VOS-028c3c-3a). Only the failure job has `issues: write`,
   declared at job level (and it needs no `contents` permission, because it does not
   check out). The actions used
   are limited to `actions/checkout`, `actions/upload-artifact`,
   `actions/download-artifact` and a Rust toolchain installer, each pinned by full commit
   SHA, and `actions/checkout` uses `persist-credentials: false`. No secrets, no push.

---

## 10. What VOS-029 must deliver

**Status after VOS-028c3c (the re-plan has run): R3, funded, provisional.** The CI
recording gives R3 with K = 2 and memory at the shipped T (section 8.6.4's decision
sentence in 8.6): VOS-029a is funded unchanged and VOS-029b is funded with sweeping as the
cut source only for entries of at most 200 instructions with equivalence points at most 2
apart; both stay provisional until the named reviewer signs (VOS-028c3d). The two
paragraphs below are history.

**Status after VOS-028c1 (the re-plan is specified, not yet run): not funded.**
VOS-029a's funding depends only on the not-R1 condition of section 8.6.4 and is
expected to be re-funded unchanged; VOS-029b depends on B5 and K, and the memory
shapes on D5 (section 8.6.4). The paragraph below is the status after VOS-028b.

**Status after VOS-028b: not funded.** The probe's decision is row 1 of section 8.3
(section 8.5), so VOS-029 is a *design for a re-plan*, not a work item: what
follows records what the route would need, and the probe's numbers say which parts
are cheap (certified per-instruction cuts, A1 and A2, a cut between accesses on small
tables) and which are not (monolithic miters from 50 instructions, self-composition
without relational cuts, a whole table compared after every access above 16 words).

VOS-029 is the symbolic RV32I executor and the first machine proof (VER13
section 4.3 item 2). It is **designed around the compositional route**, with cut
points, per-path splitting and concretised addresses from its first line, never
added as later mitigations. It is funded only if VOS-028b's decision is one of the
first two rows of section 8.3.

1. **An RV32I symbolic executor** over `riscv-simulator::decode`, with
   concrete-PC path enumeration (section 4.1), a fork limit that fails closed,
   and a **differential against the concrete simulator** (section 7): random
   states, instruction by instruction, plus the SAT-control replay.
2. **Per-path obligations plus a coverage obligation** (section 3.1): every
   path has its refinement, closure and frame obligations, and the entry has one
   coverage obligation listing every path, feasible or not.
3. **Cut-point support with certified composition.** This needs either:
   * route A of section 6.1 (a CNF per segment with the hypotheses as clauses
     and the manifest recording them), which works today; or
   * route B: `cdcl-sat` logging the failed-assumption clause as a DRAT lemma,
     bound to `(original CNF, stated clause)`, hypotheses as assumptions only,
     and checked by appending the negated clause as units and running the
     existing `drat-check` (no new accept mode; VOS-D045, VER-SAT2).
   Today a `solve_with_assumptions` UNSAT leaves no checkable lemma. The
   composition checker of section 6.2 (which regenerates CNFs and the hypothesis
   pairing and trusts no manifest field) is part of the deliverable, together with
   the model-decomposition obligation (section 3.2a), the FRAME conjunct in every
   cut relation (section 4.2), the witnesses of section 4.6 (one replayed end to
   end on the concrete simulator), and the access obligations of section 3.4.
4. **Index case-splitting** (section 4.3) and concrete frame addresses
   (section 4.4).
5. **A streaming proof sink.** `cdcl-sat` keeps the whole DRAT in memory
   (`solver.rs:375`); about 1.7 GB of resident memory per million conflicts
   makes proof memory the first resource to run out. Either the obligations stay
   under 100k conflicts (about 175 MB) or a streaming sink lands first
   (VOS-D044).
6. **`pick` proved on the compiled image at the shipped N**, with a
   **non-collapsed certificate**, a **SAT sabotage control** (a mutated image
   that must fail with a replayed counterexample) and **manifest-derived
   labels** (section 5).
7. **A re-run of probe tiers B and D on the real compiled `pick`, `insert` and
   `remove`**, replacing the proxy rows with real ones. Until then, every number in
   this document is a proxy number (the validity limits of section 8.4).

Not in VOS-029: trap-entry and CSR modelling (a later slice, with
VOS-020/VOS-021), the image checks of section 3.7 beyond `pick`'s own image,
two-safety on a real entry (VOS-026 and after), and the owned-compile lane.

---

## 11. Open questions and findings

### 11.1 Open questions

1. **Cut relation generation.** Cuts are easy at source-aligned points and hard
   at arbitrary instruction boundaries of compiled code. Which `rustc` output
   shapes admit a clean `R`? The probe cannot answer it (synthetic code);
   VOS-029 will, per entry.
2. **Does `alpha` itself fit a cone?** `alpha` over a 64-entry table is itself a
   circuit with table structure. The calibration did not measure the model-side
   cost separately.
3. **Memory models.** Byte-addressed loads and stores (`lbu`, `sb`) into a
   word-organized table add alignment cases the probe covers only
   lightly (D1 uses word accesses; `lbu`/`sb` appear in A and B).
4. **Incremental versus per-CNF.** If route B lands, is one incremental certified
   solver faster than many CNFs, or does the learned-clause database of an
   incremental run slow later segments? **Answered in part by B3 (VOS-028b):** at
   1,000 per-instruction segments one incremental solver was about forty times
   slower than a fresh CNF per segment (about two minutes against about three
   seconds); the incremental mode logs no proof, so route B's certified form is
   still unmeasured.
5. **Equivalence sweeping.** VOS00 section 12 row 9 (arrays, incremental solving,
   k-induction, and the planned equivalence-sweeping work) would make the
   monolithic route cheaper. The probe's decision table keeps it as the
   `Infeasible` row's escape hatch. **VOS-028c measures it as B5** (section 8.6.3,
   planned, VOS-028c3), including the `fuse` knob that makes it a test and not a
   tautology; the outcome is rows R2 to R4 of section 8.6.4.
6. **The owned-compile lane.** If the decision is the third row, the lane with a
   source map (VER13 section 4.3 item 1) is the way to get cuts by construction.
   Its cost (a new back end) is outside this program's current plan.

### 11.2 Findings logged in the backlog

The advisor work found six things that the VOS backlog now holds as discovered
work (VOS-D042 to VOS-D047); the ones that belong to the VER program's own
backlog are cross-referenced there.

| Finding | Row |
|---|---|
| `drat-check`'s default limits reject mid-sized proofs (2^32 work at about 105k to 237k conflicts); per-obligation budget must be set below that; raising is a reviewed decision only | VOS-D042 |
| Random straight-line programs are not a valid benchmark: they lose entropy and the miter collapses; liveness guards are mandatory | VOS-D043 (a guard of VOS-028b, section 9.4 G2) |
| Proof memory is the first resource to run out; a streaming proof sink is a likely VOS-029 prerequisite | VOS-D044 |
| `cdcl-sat` lacks certified assumption-based UNSAT (failed-assumption lemma logging) for cut-point composition | VOS-D045 |
| No `riscv32` target is installed and VOS22 does not exist, so capacity numbers are proxies until VOS-029 re-runs them | VOS-D046 |
| VOS00 section 10.2 claimed fixed-size arrays are "within reach of inlining plus bit-blasting"; true only for concrete or case-split indices | VOS-D047, done in this PR |

VOS-028b added six more (VOS-D091 to VOS-D096) and measured VOS-D042:

| Finding | Row |
|---|---|
| The default `drat-check` work limit rejects a proof from 82,835 conflicts and has accepted one of 457,844 (measured) | VOS-D042 |
| A generated harness installs its allocation observer as the global allocator when included in another program | VOS-D091 |
| The probe's resident-set poll spawns `ps` every 50 ms | VOS-D092 |
| D instances share one symbolic base index; independent indices and the out-of-range case are not measured | VOS-D093 |
| A full run writes its file only at the end (a first 2 h 17 min run was lost to a wrong provenance field) | VOS-D094 |
| The 120 s row wall bounds the sum of a chain's obligations; the 8.3 feasibility total is 10 minutes | VOS-D095 |
| Two-safety needs relational cut points, a split with chained updates and a whole-table comparison need a cone-of-influence comparison: the two shapes that put 8.5 in row 1 | VOS-D096 |
